ሱስም ፡ እምሐቅለ ፡ ቤተ ፡ህሩም ። ወኮነ ፡ ረድኦ ፡ ለኤልያስ ፡ ነቢይ ፡ ዘየዓቢ ። ወተዓገሠ ፡ 'በእንቲአሁ ፡ ላዕለ ፤ መከራ ፡ ብዙኅ፥ ፡ 'ወድኅነ ፡ እምኔሆሙ፥ ። ወውእቱ ፡ መኩንነ ፡ ዛሣልሳይ ፡ ዘመሐኮ ፡ ኤልያስ ፡ ነቢይ ። ወወረደ ፡ ወሖረ ፡ ምስሌሁ ፡ ኅበ ፡ አካዝ ፡ ንጉሥ ። ወእምድኅረዝ ፡ ኀደገ ፡ መልእክተ ፡ ንጉሠ ፡ ምድር ፡ ወተነበየ፤ ። ወሶበ ፡ ሞተ ፡ ተቀብረ ፡ ምስለ።" ፡ አበዊሁ ። ጸሎቱ ፡ ወበረከቱ ፡ የሀሉ ፡ ምስሌነ ፡ አሜን ። ወበዛቲ ፡ ዕለት ፡ ካዕበ ፡ ተቀደሰት ፡ ቤተ ፡ ክርስቲያና ፡ ለመሪና ። በረከታ ፡ የሀሉ ፡ ምስለ ፡ ፍቁሩ ፡ ዘመንፈስ ፡ ቅዱስ ። አመ ፡ ፳፪ለኅዳር ፡ ምንባብ ። በስመ ፡ አብ ፡ ወወልድ ፡ ወመንፈስ ፡ ቅዱስ ፡ አሐዱ ፡፣ አምላክ ። በዛቲ፣ዕለት፤፣ተዝካሮሙቶ፥፡ለ፳ወ፪'ካህናት ፡ሰማያዊያን፤ ፡እለ ፡ይነብሩ፣ አውደ ፡ 'መንበር ፡ ኪሩባዊሁ ፡ ለእግዚአብሔር ። እሉ ፡ መንፈሳዊያን ፡ እለ ፡ አል ቦሙ ፡ ሥጋ ፡ ካህናተ ፡ ጽድቅ ፡ በአማን ። እስመ ፡ እሙንቱ ፡ ይትሌዓሉ ፡ እምኩ ሎሙ ፡ ቅዱሳን ፡ ወእምሰራዊት ፡ መንፈሳዊያን ። 'እስመ ፡ እሙንቱ ፡ ቅሩባን ፡ በኅበ ፡ እግዚአብሔር ፡ ይተነብሉ ፡ በእንተ ፡ ዘመደ ፡ እጓለ ፡ እመሕያው ፡ ወያቀርቡ ፡ ሎቱ ፡ ጸሎታቲሆሙ ፡ ለቅዱሳን ፡ ከመ ፡ ዕጣን ፡፤ ውስተ ፤ ማዕጠንታት ፡ በእደዊሆሙ" ። ወኢያዓርግ ፡ ጽድቅ ፡ ወኢምጽዋት ፡ ኅበ ፡ እግዚአብሔር ፡፡'ዘእንበለ ፡ ቦሙ፤ ፡ በከመ፣ ይቤ ፡ ቅዱስ ፡ ዮሐንስ ፡ ወንጌላዊ ፡ በራእዩ ፡ አቡ ፡ ቀለምሲስ ። ይቤ፤፤ ፡ እስመ ፡ አነ ፡ ርኢኩ ፡ መንበረ!" ፡ ፳ወ8፪አረጋዊያን"" ፡ የ[ዐ]ውድዎ ፡ እንዘ ፡ ይነብሩ ፡ ዲበ ፡ ፳ወ፪መናብርት ፡ ወዲበ ፡ አርእስቲሆሙ ፡ ፳ወ፪ አክሊላት ፡ ወበእደዊሆሙ ፡ ፳ወ፪ማዕጠንት ፤ ውስቴቶሙ፤፥፤ ፡፣ ዕፍረት ፡ ዕጣኖሙ ፡ እለ ፡ አሙንቱ ፡ ጸሎታቲሆሙ ፡ ለቅዱሳን ፡ እለ ፡ ይነብሩ ፡ 'ዲበ ፡ ምድር፤ ፡ 'ያዓርግዋ ፡ ለእግዚአብ ሔር!" ፡ አኀዜ ፡ ኩሉ፤ ። ወይቤ ፡ ሰማዕኩ?" ፡ 8እንስሳ ፡ ወሰብሑ ፡ እንዘ ፡ ይብሉ፣ ቅዱስ ፡ ቅዱስ ፡ ቅዱስ ፤ እግዚአብሔር ፡ ጸባኦትቶ" ፡ ምሉእ ፡ ሰማያተ ፡ ወምድረ ፡ ቅድሳተ ፡ ስብሐቲከ ። ወሶቤሃ፤፤ ፡ ይሰግዱ ፡ ፳ ወ8፪ካህናት፤፡ ፡ በገጾሙ ፡ ወይከልኡ ፡ አክሊላቲ[ሆ]ሙ ፡ ወይብሉ ፡ አንተ ፡ ውእቱ ፡ ዘይደልወከ ፡ ስብሐት ፡ ወክብር ፡ ወአኩቴት ። ወሶበ ፡ ይወፅእ ፡ ትእዛዝ ፡ እምኅበ ፡ እግዚአብሔር ፡ 'አምላክ ፡ አኃዜ ፡ ዙሉ ፡ ይሰግዱ ፡ ካዕበ ፤ በገጾሙ ፡ እንዘ ፡ ይብሉ ፡ ለከ ፡ ስብሐት ፡ ወክሂል ፡ ወኩነኔከ ፡ ኩነኔ" ፡ ጽድቅ ፡ ኦአምላክነ ። ወመምህራነ ፡ ቤተ ፡ ክርስቲያንሰ፤" ፡
of Bëta-herum (= Beth-Ephraim?). He was the disciple of 'Elyäs (= Elijah) the great prophet. He endured for him many trials and was delivered from them. He was the third captain of fifty whom 'Elyäs the prophet spared. He went down and went with him to the king 'Akäz (= Ahab). After that, he abandoned the service of the king of the earth and prophesied. When he died, he was buried with his parents. May his prayer and his blessing be with us, amen. [On this day also was consecrated the church of Marinä (= Marina). May her blessing be with his dear Za-Manfas Qeddus.] [READING] FOR THE 24TH OF HEDAR In the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit, one God. On this day (there is) the commemoration of the twenty-four heavenly priests who are seated around the cherubic throne of God, those spiritual (beings) who do not have bodies, priests of justice truly. Assuredly, they are raised above all the saints and the spiritual armies. Because they are close to God, they intercede for the human race and present to him (= God) the prayers of the saints, like incense, in censers (that are) in their hands. Justice and alms only rise to God through them, according to what the saint Yohannes (= John) the evangelist says in his vision, the Ibugalamsis (= Apocalypse); he says: I saw a throne, twenty-four elders surrounded it seated on twenty-four thrones. On their heads (there were) twenty-four crowns, in their hands twenty-four censers; in these (there was) the perfume of their incense which is the prayers of the saints who dwell on earth, they make it rise toward God almighty. He says: "I heard four living creatures and they praised (God) saying: Holy, holy, holy (is) the God of armies. The heavens and the earth are full of the holiness of your glory." Then the twenty-four priests prostrated themselves on their faces, took off their crowns, and said: "It is you who are worthy of glory, of honor, and of praise." When an order emanates from the Lord, the God almighty, they also prostrate themselves on their faces saying: "To you (are) the glory and the

በእንተ ፡ ዘረከቡ ፡ ውስተ ፡ መጻሕፍት ፡ ቅዱሳት ፡ ጽሑፍ ፣ በእንቲአሆሙ ፡ ወዘረከቡ ፡ ጽሑፍ ፡ እምዜናሆሙ ፡ ለሐዋርያት ፡ ወቀኖናሆሙ ፡ በእንተ ፡ እሉ ፡ ካህናት ፡፤ ሰማያዊ[ያ]ን ፤ እስመ ፤ እሙንቱ ፡ ቅሩባን ፡ በኀበ ፣ እግዚአብሔር፥" ፡ ወዘይገብር5፤ ፣፡ ተዝካሮሙ ፡ ወ[ይ]ከሥት ፤ አስማቲሆሙ ፡ በዲበ ፡ ምድር ፡ እሙንቱ ፡ ይስእሉ ፡ 'ኀበ ፡ እግዚአብሔርቶ ፡ በእንቲአሁ፥ ፡ ወይስሪ ፡ ሎቱ ፡ ኃጢአቶ ። ወበእንተዝ ፡ ሠርዑቶ ፡ መምህራነ ፡ ቤተ ፡ ክርስቲያን ፡ 'ዘንተ ፡ በዓለ፤ ፡ በእንተ ፡ ተዝካሮሙ ፡ ለ፳ወ፪'ካህናት ፡ ሰማያዊያን፥ ። ትንብልናሆሙ ፣፤ ወጸሎቶሙ ፡ ወበረከቶሙ ፡ የሀሉ ፡ ምስሌነ ፡ ለዓለመ ፡ ዓለም ፡ አሜን ፡ ወአሜን ።
power, your judgment (is) a just judgment, our God." The doctors of the Church, because of what they found written about them in the holy Scriptures and (of) what they found written in the accounts of the apostles and their rules regarding these heavenly priests - namely: "they are close to God and whoever will make their commemoration and reveal their names on earth, for him they pray to God and his sin will be forgiven him" -, because of that (therefore), the doctors of the Church established this feast for the commemoration of the twenty-four spiritual priests. May their intercession, their prayer, and their blessing be with us in the ages of ages, amen and amen.

On this day also became a martyr the saint 'Asqir, the priest of Nägrän (= Najran), (as well as) thirty-eight martyrs who (were) with him, in the days of the reign of Saräbhël (= Sarahbiil), king of Hamir (= Himyar). (The latter) ordered 'Azqir to be brought to him, to be put in prison, and to close the door on him. || ordered the prison guards not to let anyone in. When the saint 'Azqir had prayed, the doors of the prison opened and fifty men entered to him. He made a cistern there and baptized them in the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit, one God. When the king Saräbhël learned (this), he ordered them (= the guards) to take him out of the prison. He ordered him to be taken to another place; a man whose name (was) Kiryäq (= Cyriacus) met him and said to him: "Happy news for you, 'Azqir: you are being led to martyrdom!" Hearing (this), the king's soldiers bound Kiryäq with him. On the way, two men met him and said to him: "Baptize us for Christ." 'Asqir prayed to God, (God) made water gush from (this) desolate place and ('Asqir) baptized them with Kiryäq. Arrived in the desert, they (= the soldiers) lacked water as well as their beasts. They begged the saint 'Asqir to pray to God for them regarding the lack of water. 'Azqir prayed to God and, immediately, a cloud arrived and made water rain into a basin to (a depth of) a palm; from this water seven hundred soldiers and their beasts drank to satiety. (The soldiers) brought 'Azqir to the king of 'Amir. (The latter) said to him: "What is this new doctrine that you have brought into our country?" The saint 'Azqir said: "This is not a new doctrine, but that which the prophets preached in the Torah." Upon hearing (this), the king of the Jews mocked the faith of the Christians. One of the rabbis stood up and said to the king: "My lord, order him to be taken to his city, to be hung on a tree, and to be burned alive with fire." The king gave orders according to his (= the rabbi's) words. When he had been brought to the city of Nägrän, he was hung on a tree and fire was lit under him. When the saint 'Azqir had prayed, his bonds were undone and he came out alive from the midst of the fire. The Jews said to each other again: "Come, let us stone him." When they

stoned him, the stones returned and killed most of the Jews. Those who remained took a sword and cut off the head of the saint 'Azqir. || finished (thus) his martyrdom and went into eternal life. May his holy blessing be with our king 'Iyäsu and with his dear Za-Manfas Qeddus in the ages of ages.] [On this day also (there is) the commemoration of Qalqew, Gäyos, 'Atreyos, and (of) Diyosqoros (= Dioscorus) (whose) history and mention (are) sweet, and (of) Diyosqoros the martyr (and) (spiritual) combatant. May their blessing be with us, amen.] On this day also died Abbä Yosëf (= Joseph) of the city of Dän (= Tanis?). The parents of this saint were rich in faith and had no (other) child than him. They raised him with a fine education and taught him the writings of the Church. His appearance was like (that of) Yosëf, the son of Yä'qob (= Jacob). When he had grown, the memory of death and the fragility of this world entered his heart. He went into a monastery, became a monk, and became a combatant (spiritual) assiduous in fasting and prayer. He would close his dwelling and would only go out on Sunday at the time of the Eucharist. When his fame became known, the people of the region came to him to receive his blessing. As for his father and his mother, as they had no news of him, they remained begging that God might make them meet him. Having learned of his fame, they went to him so that he might pray for them and that they might find their son again. When they arrived to him, he recognized them, but they did not recognize him. After the people had left, he made himself known to them and ordered them not to speak (of it) to people, and he encouraged them and sent them back in peace. God gave him the gift of performing miracles and wonders so that Satan was jealous of him. He went to the governor of the city and told him that this saint had riches. The governor sent soldiers, (had) this saint tied up, and had him put in prison. The next day, when he (went) to see him, (his) bonds were found lying on the ground. The governor trembled, prostrated himself at his feet, submitted humbly to him, and sent him back to his dwelling. After many (spiritual) combats, he died in peace. There appeared from his tomb miracles and wonders without number. May his blessing be with our king 'Iyäsu and with his dear Za-Manfas Qeddus in the ages of ages.]

### READING FOR THE 25TH OF HEDAR

In the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit, one God. On this day became a martyr the saint Pilupädër (= Philopator), which, in

ቅዱስ ፡ ኮነ ፡ እምሰብአ ፡ ሀገረ ፡ አስሌጥስ ፡ እንተ ፡ ይእቲ ፡ሀገረ ፡ አቡሁ ፡ 'ወአበ ፡ አቡሁ ፡ ዘተወልደ ፡ ባቲ ፡ ወተሐጽነ ፡ በሀ[ገ]ረ ፡ ሮሜ ። ወኮነ ፡ ግብሮሙ ፡ ለአበ ዊሁ ፡ ያሠግሩ ፡ አራዊተ ። ወበአሐቲ ፡ ዕለት ፡ ወፅኡ ፡ በከመ ፡ ልማዶሙ ፡'ከመ ፡ ያሥግሩ ፡ ወረከብዎሙ ፡ ፪ፅደወ ፡ እምገጻተ ፡ ከለባት ፡ ወበልዑ ፡'አበ ፡ አቡሁ ፡ ወፈቀዱ ፡ ከመ ፡ ይብልዑ ፡ አቡሁ ። ወከልዖሙ ፡ መልአከ ፡ እግዚአብሔር ፡'እንዘ ፡ ይብል! ፡ ኢትግሥሥዎ፤! ፡ እስመ ፡ እምኔሁ ፡ ይወፅእ ፡ ፍሬ ፡ ሠናይ ። ወእምዝ ፡ ዓገቶሙ ፡ መልአከ ፡ እግዚአብሔር ፡ 'በዓውደ ፡ እሳት፤ ። ወሶበ ፡ ተመንደቡ ፡ መጽኡ ፡ ኅበ ፡ አቡሁ ፡ ለቅዱስ ፡ መርቆርዮስ፤ ፡ ወሰገዱ ፡ ሎቱ ። ወወለጠ ፥፡ እግዚአብሔር ፡ ፍጥረቶሙ ፡ ኀበ ፡ የውሃት ፡ ወኮኑ ፡ ከመ ፡ አባግዕ ፡ ወቦኡ ፡ ምስሌሁ ፡ ውስተ ፡ሀገር! ። ወእምድኅረዝ ፡ ወለዶ ፡ ለቅዱስ፤፤ ፡ ወሰመዮ ፡ ፒሉፓዴር ። ወገጸጻተ ፡ ከለባትሰ ፡ ነበሩ ፡ በኀቤሆሙ ፡ ብዙኃ ፡ መዋዕለ ፡ ወኮኑ! ፡ ክርስቲያነ ። እስመ ፡ አበዊሁ ፡ ለዝንቱ ፡ ቅዱስ ፡ ኮኑ፤ ፡ አረማዊያነ ፡ ወሶበ ፡ ነሥኡ ፡ ጸጋ፤ ፡ ጥምቀተ ክርስትና ፡ ሰመይዎ ፡ ለአቡሁ ፡ ኖኅ ፡ ወለእሙ ፡ ታቦት ፡ ወለፒሎፓዴር ፡ መርቆር ዮስ፤-፤፡9 ። በከመ ፡ ይቤሎሙ ፡ መልአክ ፡ ሶበ ፡ አስተርአዮሙ ፡ ወኮኑ ፡ ገጻተ ፡ ከለ ባት ፤ ይትኤዘዙ ፡ ለአቡሁ ፡ ለቅዱስ ፣ መርቆርዮየ[ስ]፤፤ ። ወሰምዓ ፡ ንጉሥ ፡ በእንቲአሆሙ ፤ ፡ ወአብጽሆሙ ፡ ኀቤሁ ። ወሜጠ ፤ ሎሙ ፡ እግዚአብሔር ፡ ፍጥረቶሙ ፡ ለአራዊት ፡ በቅድመ ፡ ንጉሥ ፡ ወአጥፍእዎሙ ፡ ለኩሎሙ ፡ አራዊት ፡እለ፣ አቅረበ ፡ ሎሙ ፡ ንጉሥ ። ወሶበ ፡ርእየ ፡ዘንተ ፡ ፈርሃ ፡ ፈድፋደ ። ወሰአሎ ፡ ንጉሥ ፡ ለአቡሁ ፡ ለቅዱስ ፡ መርቆርዮስ ፡ ከመ ፡ ይስአሎ ፡ ለእግዚአብሔር ፡ ከመ ፡ ያእትት ፡ እምኔሆሙ ፡፤ ፍጥረቶሙ ፡ ለአራዊት ፡ ወይሚጦሙ ፡ ከመ ፡ ፍጥረተ ፡ እጓለ ፡ እመሕያው" ። ወእምድኅረዝ ፡ ነሥኦ ፡ ለአቡሁ ፡ ለቅዱስ ፡ መርቆርዮስ?" ፣ ወሜሞ ፡ መኩንነ ፡ ወሊቀ ፡ ሰራዊት ። ወኮኑ ፡ እሉንቱ ፡ ገጻተ ፡ ከለባት ፡ ይትኤዘዙ ፡ ሎቱ ፡ ወኮኑ ፡ ኩሎሙ ፡ ሰብእ ፡ ይፈርሁ ፡ እምኔሆሙ ። ወእምድኅረዝ ፡ ተንሥአ ፡ ንጉሥ ፡ ዓላዊ ፡ ወፈቀደ ፡ ይፃብኦ ፡ ለንጉሥ ። ወፈነዎሙ ፡ ንጉሥ ፡ 'ለሐራሁ ፡ ከመ ፡ ይጊጥ ዎሙ ፡ ለእሉ ፡ ገጸተ ፡ ከለባት ፡ ወያምጽእዎሙ”። ፡ ኅቤሁ ፡ ወጌጦሙ ፡ ንጉሥ፥"፣ ዘፈቀደ ፡ ከመ ፡ ይጻብኦ ፡ ለንጉሠ ፡ ሀገር ፡ ዘውስቴታ0፤ ፡ አቡሁ?" ፡ ለቅዱስ ፡ መርቆርዮስ፥ ። 'ወበጽሑ ፤ ኀቤሁ ፡ ገጻተ ፡ ከለባት ፡ ወኩነኖ ፡ ለ፳እምኔሆሙ ፡ ወኮነ ፡ ሰማዕተ ፡ ወካልኡ ፡ ጐየ ። ወሶበ ፡ ተመይጠ ፡ አቡሁ ፡ ለቅዱስ ፡ መርቆርዮስ፤ ፡ ኀበ ፡ ሀገር ፡ ኀሠሦ ፡ ለወልዱ ፡ መርቆርዮስ፥ ፡ ወኢረከቦ ፡ ለእሙ ፡ ካዕበ ። እስመ ፡ ንጉሥ ፡ ሶበ ፡ ሰምዓ ፡ ከመ ፡ አቡሁ ፡ ለቅዱስ ፡ መርቆርዮስ" ፡ ተቀትለ ፡ በውስተ ፡ ፀብእ ፡ ሐለየ ፡ በልቡ ፡ ከመ ፡ ይንሥኣ ፡ ለብእሲቱ ፡ እሞ ፡ ለቅዱስ ፡ መርቆርዮስ፥ ፡ ወያውስባ፥፤ ። ወሶበ ፡ አእመረ ፡ 8እምላእካኒሁ ፡ ለንጉሥ ፡ ዘሃለየ ፡ ንጉሥ ፡ ሖረ ፡ ወነገራ ፡ ለእሙ ፡ ለቅዱስ ፡ መርቆርዮስ። ፡ በዘ ፡ ሀለየ ፡ ንጉሥ ፡ በእንቲአሃ ። ወሰአለቶ ፡ ከመ ፣ ያውጽኣ ፡ እምሀገር ፡ በኅቡእ ፡ ወወፅአት ፡ ይእቲ ፡ ወወልዳ ፡ ብፁዕ ፡ መርቆርዮስ ። ወሶበ ፣ ኀሠሠ ፡ አቡሁ ፡ ለቅዱስ ፡ ብአሲቶ ፡
translation, (means) "who loves his father", Marquoryos (= Mercurius), which, also in translation, (means) "servant of Jesus Christ". This saint was from the people of the city of 'Aslëtes, which was the city of his father and of his father's father, in which he was born, and he was raised in the city of Rome. The trade of his parents (= his father and his grandfather) was to hunt wild animals. One day, they went out as was their custom to hunt and met two men (who were part) of the cynocephali; they devoured his grandfather and wanted to devour his father. An angel of God prevented them (from doing so), saying: "Do not touch him, for, from him, will come a beautiful fruit." Then the angel of God surrounded them with a circle of fire. As they were tormented, they came near the father of the saint Margoryos and prostrated themselves before him. God changed their nature into gentleness and they were like lambs and entered with him into the city. After that, he begot the saint and called him Pilupädër. As for the cynocephali, they remained with them for many days and became Christians. Now, the parents of this saint had been pagans; when they received the grace of Christian baptism, his father was called Noh (= Noah) and his mother, Täbot and Pilopädër, Margoryos as the angel had told them when he had appeared to them; the cynocephali obeyed the father of the saint Margoryos. The emperor heard (about) them, he had them brought to him. God made the nature of wild animals return for them before the emperor and they destroyed all the wild animals that the emperor had brought near them. When (the emperor) saw that, he was very afraid. The emperor begged the father of the saint Margoryos to pray to God so that he might take away from them the nature of wild animals and change them (so that they would have a nature) like the nature of man. After that, he took the father of the saint Margoryos and named him governor and army chief. The cynocephali obeyed him and all men feared them. After that, an unfaithful king arose and wanted to make war on the emperor. The (unfaithful) king sent his soldiers to persuade the cynocephali and bring them to him. The king who wanted to make war on the emperor of the city in which (was) the father of the saint Margoryos persuaded them. The cynocephali came to him; (the king) tortured one of them, and he became a martyr, the other fled. When the father of the saint Margoryos returned to the city, he looked for his son Margoryos and did not find him (nor) his mother either. Indeed, the emperor, when he had heard (it said) that the father of the saint Margoryos had been killed in the war, had meditated in his heart to take his wife, the mother of the saint Margoryos, and

to marry her. When one of the emperor's servants knew what the emperor was meditating, he went and told the mother of the saint Margoryos what the emperor was meditating regarding her. She asked him to take her out of the city in secret, and she went out, she and her son, the blessed Margoryos. When the father of the saint looked for his wife and his son, he did not find them and no one let him know what had become of them. The emperor was afraid of him (fearing) that the cynocephali were hidden with him, that he might have them come and destroy the whole city. The emperor ordered not to tell him what he had meditated regarding his wife, the mother of the saint Margoryos. After that, war broke out against the emperor. The father of the saint Margoryos went to war and he was taken. By the will of God, the emperor of the city of Rome was a Christian. When he learned that the father of the saint Margoryos (was) a Christian, he spared him, did not kill him, and named him governor of the land of the Mardesäwiyän. By the will of God, the saint Margoryos and his mother were in the city of Rome. When his father came to the church, his wife, the mother of the saint Margoryos, saw him and recognized that it was her husband. One day, when he and his soldiers went out before the inn where they were staying, the mother of the blessed Margoryos took him, dressed him in his beautiful clothes that he wore in the royal city, and ordered him to go and mount the horse of the governor, that is to say his father. When he was mounted on the horse, his (= his father's) soldiers seized him and brought him before his father; (the latter) did not know that it was his son and he became angry with him. The mother of Margoryos came to her husband and he did not know that it was his wife. She said to him: "Our lord, we are strangers and, when we knew that you were a stranger, I thought that my son (could) be with you." He questioned and interrogated her, and she told him that she was his wife. Immediately, he recognized her and he recognized his son Margoryos. || made the inn a church and they remained there together. When his father and his mother died, the emperor took the saint Margoryos and named him governor of the land of the Mardesäwiyän in the place of his father. The cynocephali had remained until the saint Margoryos was named governor. The cynocephali would go with him to war. When he had need of them, God would return to them their nature of wild animals as before and no one could resist them. It was given to this saint power and great strength and he was called Margoryos by the people of the palace. In those days, the emperor of the city of Rome was Däkiyos (= Decius) who worshipped idols. Enemies (who were part) of the barbarians rose against him. || gathered his army and went to fight with them. Here they

መልአከ ፡ እግዚአብሔር ፡ ወበእዴሁ ፡ ሰይፍ ፡ በሊሕ ፡ ወወእቱ ፡ ይቤሎ ፡ ሞእ ፡ ወኢትፍራህ ፡ ኦመርቆርዮስ ። ወእምድኅረዝ ፡ አስተርአዮ ፡ ሎቱ" ፡ 'ብእሲ ፣ ብሩህ፤ ፡ በአልባስ ፡ ጸዓዳ ፡ ወበእዴሁ ፡ ሰይፍ" ፡ ወወሀቦ ፡ ሎቱ ፡ ኪያሁ ፡ እንዘ ፡ ይብል ፤ ሶበ ፤ ትመውዖሙ ፡ ለጸላእትከ ፡ ተ[ዘ]ከሮ ፡ ለእግዚአብሔር ፡፤ አምላክከ ። ወሶበ ፣ ሞኦሙ ፡ ለጸላእቱ ፡ ወገብአ ፡ 'በመዊእ ፡ ወበፍሥሐ ፡ ወአስተርአዮ ፡ መልአክ ፡ ብሩህ ፡ ወይቤሎ ፡ ለምንት ፡ ረሣዕከ ፡ ወኢተዘከርከ ፡ ስሞ ፡ ለእግዚአብሔር ፡ አምላክከ ። ወሶበ ፡ ተፈጸመ ፡ ጊዜ ፡ ፀብእ ፡ ወፈቀደ ፡ ዳኪዮስ ፡ ንጉሥ ፡ ከመ ፡ ይውግር፤5' ፡ ፅጣነ ፡ ለጣዖታቲሁ፤ ፡ ውእቱ ፡ ወሰራዊቱ ። ወጐንደየ ፡ ቅዱስ ፡ መርቆርዮስ ፡ ወኢመጽአ' ፡ ምስለ ፡ ንጉሥ ፡ ኀበ ፡ ቤተ ፤ ጣዖታት ። 'ወሶበ ፣ አጠየቅዎ፤ ፡ ለንጉሥ ፡ ዳኪዮስ። ፡ ወይቤልዎ ፡ እስመ ፡ መርቆርዮስ” ፡ ኢመጽአ ፡ ምስለ ፡ ሰራዊት ፡ ወኢተደመረ ፡ ለውግረተ ፡ዕጣን ፡ ለአማልክት ። ወፈነወ ፡ ንጉሥ፣ "ወአብጽሖ ፡ ለቅዱስ ፡ መርቆርዮስ ፡ ወአንከረ ፡ በእንተ ፡ ኃዲጎቱ" ፡ ፍቅረ ፡ ዚአሁ ። ወይቤሎ ፡ ለምንት ፡ ተረሥዓከ” ፡ ፍቅረ ፡ ዚአየ ፡ ለከ! ፡ ወኢመጻጸእከ ፡ ምስሌየ ፡ ከመ ፡ ትውግር ፡ ዕጣነ ፡ ለአማልክት ። ወሶቤሃ ፡ ወረወ ፡ ሎቱ ፡ ቅዱስ ፡ መርቆርዮስ፤‹፤ ፡ ቅናቶ ፡ ወልብሶ ፡ "ውስተ ፡ ገጹ ፡ ለንጉሥ፤። ፡ ዳኪዮስ፤0ቦ ፡ ወይ ቤሎ ፡ አንሰ ፤ ኢይክሕዶ ፡ ለእግዚእየ ፡ ኢየሱስ ፡ ክርስቶስ!0 ፣ ወኢይስግድ ፡፤ ለጣዖታት ፡ ርኩሳት ። ወሶበ ፡ ሰምዓ ፡ ዳኪዮስ፤ ፡ ንጉሥ ፡ ዘንተ ፡ ተምዓ ፡ ፈድፋደ ፡ ወአዘዘ ፡ ከመ ፡ ይዝብጥዎ ፡ በአብትር ፡ 'ሐመልሚል ፡ ዘበቀልት፤ ፡ ወበጥብጣቤ ፡ አልህምት ፡ ወገብሩ ፡ ቦቱ ፡ዘንተ፤" ። ወሶበ፤ ፡ ፈርሃ ፡ ንጉሥ ፡ ከመ ፡ ይትነሥኡ ፡ ላዕሌሁ ፡ ሰብአ ፡ ሀገር ፡ በእን[ቲ]አሁ ፡ ሞቅሖ ፡ በሐጺን፤‹ ፡ ወፈነዎ ፡ ኅበ ፡ ሀገረ ፡ ቂሳርያ ፡ ዘቀጳዶቅያ ፡ ወአዘዘ ፡ ከመ ፡ ይኩንንዎ ፡ በህየ ፡ ከመ ፡ ይምትሩ ፡ ርእሶ፤!‹ ። ወኩነንዎ ፡'ዓቢየ ፡ ኩነኔ፤፤፤ ፡ በከመ ፡ አዘዘ ፤፤። ፡ ወመተሩ ፡ ርእሶ ፡ በሰይፍ ። ወፈ ጸመ ፡ 'ተጋድሎቶ ፡ ሠናየ፤! ፡ ወነሥአ ፡ አክሊለ ፡ ስምዕ ፡ በመንግሥተ ፡ ሰማያት ። ወእምድኅረ ፡ ነሥአ ፡ አክሊለ ፡ ሰማያዌ ፡ ዘኢይማስን ፡ ወቦአ ፡ ውስተ ፡ ሕይወት ፡ ዘለዓለም ፡፤ ወተሐንጸ ፡ ሎቱ ፡ አብያተ ፡ ክርስቲያናት ፡ ውስተ ፡ ኩሉ ፡ አጽናፈ ፡ ምድ ር፤1-115 « ወከሠተ ፡ እግዚአብሔር ፡ ውስቴቶሙ ፡ ተኣምራተ ፡ ወመንክራተ፤፤' ፡ ዓበይተ ። ወቦ ፡ እመንክራቲሁ ፡ ሶበ ፡ ነግሠ ፡ ሉልያኖስ፤ ፡ ወክህዶ ፡ ለእግዚእነ፤፤፤ ፡ ክርስቶስ ፣ በመዋዕሊሁ ፡ ለቅዱስ ፡ ባስልዮስ ፡ ኤኢስ ፡ ቆፅስ ፡ ዘሀገረ ፡ ቂሳርያ ፡ ዘቀጳዶቅያ ፡ በከመ ፡ ጽሑፍ ፡ ውስተ ፡ አሐቲ ፡ እምተኣምራቲሁ ፡ 'ወይእቲ ፡ ዓሥር ፡ ተአምሪሁ፤! ፡ ለቅዱስ ፡ ባስልዮስ ። እስመ ፡ ሉልዮስ፤ቦ ፡ ከሓዲ ፡ አመንደቦሙ ፡ ለእለ ፡ የአምኑ ፡ በእግዚእነ፤፤ ፡ ክርስቶስ ፡ ዓቢየ ፡ ምንዳቤ ። ወተቄጥዓ ፡ ላዕለ ፣ ቅዱስ ፡ ባስልዮስ ፡ ወሞቅሖ ። ወሶበ ፡ በዝኃ ፡ ምግባራቲሁ ፡ እኪት ፡ ላዕለ ፡ መሃይም ናን ፤ 'አስተጋብኦሙ ፡ ቅዱስ ፡ ባስልዮስ ፡ ለመሃይምናን"፡፤ ፡ ወኮነ ፡ ይጹሊ ፡ ወያስተበቀእ ፡ ኅበ ፡ እግዚአብሔር ፡ በእንተ ፡ ሉልያኖስ፤፤ ፡ ከኃዲ ፡ ወይተነብል ፡
(were) like the sand of the sea which is multiple: the emperor trembled and was afraid. As for the saint Margoryos, he saw before him in the battle an angel of God, in his hand (there was) a sharp sword and the latter said to him: "Conquer and do not fear, Margoryos." After that, a luminous man with white clothes appeared to him, a sword in his hand; he gave it to him saying: "When you have conquered your enemies, remember the Lord your God." When he had conquered his enemies and had returned, victorious and with joy, the luminous angel appeared to him and said to him: "Why have you forgotten and have not remembered the name of the Lord your God?" When the time of war was finished, the emperor Däkiyos wanted to offer incense to his idols, he and his army. The saint Margoryos delayed and did not come with the emperor to the temple of the idols. When they informed the emperor Däkiyos and (they) said to him: "Margoryos did not come with the army and did not associate himself with the offering of incense to the gods", the emperor dispatched, had the saint Margoryos brought, and was astonished that (the latter) had abandoned his affection for him. He said to him: "Why has your affection for me gone out of your memory and have you not come with me to offer incense to the gods?" Then the saint Margoryos threw his belt and his garment into the face of the emperor Däkiyos and said to him: "I will not deny my Lord Jesus Christ and I will not prostrate myself before impure idols." When the emperor Däkiyos heard that, he became very angry and ordered him to be struck with green palm branches and ox-sinews, and they did that to him. As the emperor feared that the people of the city might rise against him because of him (= Margoryos), he (had) him loaded with irons, sent him to the city of Qisäryä (= Caesarea) of Qapadoqyä (= Cappadocia), and ordered him to be tortured there to (then) cut off his head. They made him undergo great torture, as (the emperor) had ordered, and they cut off his head with the sword. He finished (thus) his beautiful (spiritual) combat and received the crown of martyrdom in the kingdom of heaven. After he had received the incorruptible heavenly crown and had entered into eternal life, churches were built for him in all the ends of the earth. God manifested great miracles and wonders there. Among his wonders, there was (one) when Lulyänos (= Julian) reigned and denied our Lord Christ in the time of the saint Bäselyos (= Basil), bishop of the city of Qisäryä of Qapadoqyä, as it is written in one of his miracles; it is the tenth miracle of the saint Bäselyos. Lulyos the apostate made those who believed in our Lord Christ undergo great torments. He

በቅዱስ ፣ መርቆርዮስ፤ ። ወሰምዓ ፣ እግዚአብሔር ፡ ጸሎቶ ፡ ወፈነወ ፡ ሰማዕቶ ፡ አቢየ ፡ ወቅዱሰ ፡ መርቆርዮስ፤፤፤፤። ። ወረገዞ ፡ ለከኃዲ ፡ ሉልያኖስ፤" ፡ በፉናት ፡ ውስተ ፡ ርእሱ ፡ ወሞተ ፡ በሞት ፡ እኩይ ፡ በውስተ ፡ ፀብእ ፡ በምድረ ፡ ፋርስ ። ወሶበ ፡ ርእየ ፡ ቅዱስ ፤ ዓቢይ፤፤ ፣ ባስልዮስ ፡ ዘንተ ፡ ራእየ ፣ ውስተ ፡ ሥዕሉ ፡ለቅዱስ ፡ መርቆርዮስ፤ ፡ ተፈሥሐ ፡ ፍሥሐ ፡ ዓቢየ ፡ ወቦአ ፡ ውስተ ፡ መካን ፡ ዘሀሎ ፡ ቦቱ ፡ ሥዕሉ፤፤0-፤3፤ ፣ ለሰማዕት ፡ 'ዓቢይ ፡ መርቆርዮስ፤ ። ወእንዘ ፡ 'ውእቱ ፡ ይሔ ሊ፤፤፤-13ሩ ፣ በእንተ ፡ ዘርእየ ፡ ወሶቤሃ ፡ ተመይጠት ፡ ሰይፉ ፡ ወኩናቱ ፡ ለቅዱስ ፤ መርቆርዮስ፤ ፡ ውስተ ፡ መካኖሙ ፡ ወእንዘ ፡ ይውሕዝ ፡ እምኔሆሙ ፡ ደም ፡ ብዙኅ ፡ እስከ ፡ መልአ ፡ መካነ ፡ ታሕተ ፡ ሥዕሉ ፡ ለቅዱስ ፡ መርቆርዮስ፤ ። ወሶቤሃ ፡ ጸርኃ ፡ ቅዱስ ፤ ባስልዮስ ፡ ወይቤ ፡ ኦሰማዕቱ ፡ ለክርስቶስ ፡ ቀተልኮኑ ፡ ለጸላኤ ፡ ጽድቅ ፥ ሉልያኖስ ። ወአጽነነ ፡ ሰማዕት ፡ መርቆርዮስ፤” ፡ ርእሶ ፡ ውስተ ፡ ሥዕሉ ፡ በቅድመ ፡ ቅዱስ፤፤ ፡ ባስልዮስ ፡ ከመ ፡ ዘይቤ ፡ ኦሆ፤ ። ወወሰከ ፡ ቅዱስ ፡ ባስልዮስ ፣ ስብሐ ቲቂሁ ፤ ለእግዚአብሔር ፡ ወክብሮ፤የ ፡ ወአኩቴቶ ። ወብዙኃት ፣ ተኣምራቲሁ"፤ ፡ "ለቅዱስ ፡ መርቆርዮስ! ፡ 'ወእምኔሆሙ ፡ እስመ፤ ፡ ፳ወሬዛ ፡ ተንበላት ፡ እም ውሉደ ፡ መሳፍንተ ፡ ሀገረ ፤ ምስር ፡ 'ኮነ ፡ ተምህረ፤ ፡ ሕገ ፡፤ ተንበላት ፡ ወመጸሕፍቲሆሙ ። ወበአሐቲ ፡ ዕለት ፡ ኃለፈ ፡ ኀበ ፡ ጽንፈ ፡ ባሕር ፡ ወረከበ ፡ ፳ብእሴ ፡ ዘኮነ ፡ ቀዳሚ ፡ ተንበላት ፡ ወተጠምቀ ፡ ጥምቀተ ፡ ክርስትና ። እንዘ፤ ፡ አኃዝዎ ፡ ሐራሁ ፡ ለንጉሥ ፡ ዘአስተዳለዉ፤ ፡ ሎቱ ፡ ዓዘቅተ ፡ ወአንደዱ ፡ ውስቴታ ፡ እሳተ ፡ ዓቢየ ፡ ከመ ፡ ያውዕይዎ ፡ ውስቴታ ፡ ወብዙኃን ፡ ሰብእ ፡ ተጋብኡ ፡ ከመ ፡ ይርአይዎ ። ወቀርበ ፡ ውእቱ ፡ ወሬዛ ፡ ተንበላት ፡ ኀበ ፡ ውእቱ ፡ ሰማዕት ፡ ዘአኃዝዎ ፡ ሐራሁ ፡ ለንጉሥ ፡ ወይቤሎ ፡ ኦብእሲ ፡ ከሓዲ ፡ ለምንት ፡ ትረውጽ ፡ ለበዊእ ፡ ውስተ ፡'እሳት ፤ በዝንቱ ፡ ዓለም፤ ፡ ወበደኃሪት ፡ ትነብር ፡ ውስተ ፡ እሳተ ፡ ገሃነም ፡ እስመ ፡ አንተ ፡ ትሬሲ፤ጾ ፡ ለእግዚአብሔር ፡ ወልደ ፡፤ ወተአምን ፡ በ፻ አማልክት ። ስማዕ ፡ እምኔየ ፡ ወኅድግ ፡ ዘንተ፤ ፣ ጽርፈተ!5የ ። ወአውሥኦ ፡ ወይቤሎ ፡ ንሕነሰ ፡ ሕዝበ ፡ ክርስቲያን ፡ ኢኮነ ፡ ከሓድያነ ፡ አላ ፡ ናመልክ ፡ ለ]አሐዱ ፡ አምላክ ፡ ውእቱ ፡ አብ ፡ ወወልድ ፡ ወመንፈስ ፡ ቅዱስ ። ወኢኮነ ፡ ወልደ ፡ ነኪረ ፡ እምእግዚአብሔር ፡ አቡሁ ፡ እስመ ፡ ውእቱ ፡ ቃሉ ፡ ወከማሁ ፡ መንፈስ ፡ ቅዱስ፤፤፤ ። ምሥጢረ ፡ ሃይማኖትነ ፡ መንክር ፡ ውእቱ ፡ ወኅቡእ ፡ እምኔክሙ ፡ እስመ ፡ ልብክሙ ፡፣ ኢይክል ፡ ጸዊሮቶ ። ወአንተ ፡ይእዜ ፡ ልብከ ፡ ጸሊም ፡ ኢበርሀ ፡ "ውስተ ፡ ልብከ፤፤። ፡ ብርሃነ ፡ ሃይማኖት ። ወአነ ፡ እኔጽር ፡ ከመ ፡ እምድኅረ ፡ ሕዳጥ ፡ መዋፅል ፡ ይቀርብ ፡ ኀቤከ ፡ ብርሃን ፡ ወይበርህ ፡ ልብከ ፡ በብርሃኑ ፡ ለክርስቶስ ። ወትትጋደል ፡ 'በእንተ ፡ ዘአነ፤ ፡ እትጋ ደል ፡ በእንተ ፣ ስሙ፤ ፡ ወትትወከፍ ፡ ዘንተ ፡ ሕማመ ፡ ዘአነ ፡ ውስቴቱ ፡ ይእዜ ። ወሶበ ፡ ሰምዓ ፡ ውእቱ ፡ ወሬዛ፤ ፡ ተንበላት ፡ ቃሎ ፡ ተምዓ ፡ ላዕሌሁ ፡ ፈድፋደ ፡ ወአውፅአ ፡ አሣእኒሁ ፡ እምእገሪሁ ፡ ወጸፍዖ ፡ ውስተ ፡ ገጹ ፡ ወውስተ ፡ አፉሁ ፡
became angry with the saint Bäselyos and imprisoned him. When he had multiplied his evil ways of acting against the faithful, the saint Bäselyos gathered the faithful and began to pray and beg God regarding Lulyänos the apostate and to ask for the intercession of the saint Margoryos. God heard his prayer and sent his great and holy martyr Margoryos. (The latter) with a spear pierced the head of the apostate Lulyänos (who) died a bad death during the war in the land of Färs (= Persia). When the great saint Bäselyos saw this vision in the image of the saint Margoryos, he felt great joy and entered into the place in which was the image of the great martyr Margoryos. While he was meditating on what he had seen, then the sword and the spear of the saint Margoryos returned to their place, much blood flowing from them to such a point that it filled the space under the image of the saint Margoryos. Then the saint Bäselyos cried out and said: "Martyr of Christ, have you killed the enemy of the truth, Lulyänos?" The martyr Margoryos inclined his head in his image before the saint Bäselyos as if he were saying yes. The saint Bäselyos redoubled his praises toward God as well as his veneration and thanksgiving. Numerous are the miracles of the saint Margoryos. Among these (there is this one): a young Muslim man (from among) the sons of the judges of the city of Mesr (= Cairo) was learning the law of the Muslims and their books. One day, he passed by the edge of the river and met a man who had been previously a Muslim and had been baptized with Christian baptism. While the king's soldiers had seized him, (they) who had prepared a pit for him and had lit a great fire in it to burn him in it, many people had gathered to see him. The young Muslim man approached the martyr whom the king's soldiers had seized and said to him: "Apostate man, why do you run to enter into the fire in this world, while in the next (world) you will remain in the fire of Gehenna, since you attribute a son to God and believe in three gods. Listen to me and abandon this blasphemy." (The man) answered him and said to him: "We, the people of the Christians, we are not infidels, but we worship a unique God. He is Father, Son, and Holy Spirit. The Son is not other than God his Father, for he is his Word, and, likewise, the Holy Spirit (is not other). The mystery of our faith is wonderful and (is) hidden from you, because your heart cannot bear it. As for you, now your heart is dark: the light of faith does not shine in your heart. But I, I see that, in a few days, the light will approach you and (that) your heart will shine with the light of Christ. You will fight for that (for which)

I, I am fighting, for his name, and you will receive this suffering in which (I am), I, now." When the young Muslim man heard his words, he became very angry with him. || took off his sandals from his feet and slapped him (with them) on the face, on the mouth, and on the head. He tormented him greatly and said to him: "What you said to me will never happen as far as I am concerned, apostate." (The man) answered and said to him: "You will have to remember what I said to you." Then they cut off the head of the man and threw his body into the pit of fire. After the flame of the fire had risen, it was like a great wall. (The man) remained in the fire three days and three nights and the king's soldiers guarded him day and night. After that, his body was found like pure gold, the fire had not touched it at all. They told this to the king and he ordered the body to be buried. As for the young Muslim man, he went to his house, afflicted, and did not eat or drink. His father and his brothers gathered to him and said to him: "What has happened to you? Why do you not eat and do not drink?" He told them how the martyr of our Lord Christ had spoken to him. They consoled him and said to him: "Remove from your heart the words of this deceiver", but he was not consoled at all. In those days, the young Muslim man saw many Muslims, they wanted to go to Makkä (= Mecca) (to) the tomb of their false prophet. He said to his father: "I want to go with them." His father rejoiced at that, he gave him one hundred dinars, entrusted him to a friend of his, and they left. While they were going, the young man saw, one night, like an old luminous monk, (the latter) stood near him and said to him: "Come, follow me to be saved." And he appeared to him in the same way the second night and the third night and spoke to him in the same way. When (the pilgrims) had finished what they had to do in Makkä, they returned, walked six or seven days, and arrived at the desert. While they were walking at night, the young man got off his camel for a physical need. His companions left him in the desert and went away. He remained alone, lost in the desert, and remained weeping, fearing that the animals of the desert would devour him. At that moment, he remembered the church of the holy martyr Margoryos which (was) in the city of Mesr (and) which (was) close to his father's house; he said in his heart: "All men address vows to him and he accomplishes for them everything for which they pray to him." Then the young man said weeping: "Margoryos, martyr of Christ, if you save me from the animals of the desert and make me come out (of the desert) without harm, I, I will become a Christian." When he had said that, here a young man came to him, mounted on a horse; his appearance (was) very beautiful, he was dressed in precious clothes and was girded with a

ተጸዓን ፡ ድኅሬየ፤ ፡ ዲበ ፡ ዛቲ ፡ ፈረስ ። ወተጽዕነ ፡ ድኅሬሁ! ፡ ወሰረረ ፡ ቦቱ፤፤ ፡ ፈረስ ፤ ውስተ ፡ አየር ፡ ወአብጽሖ፤ ፡ ፍጡነ ፡ ኅበ ፡ ቤተ ፡ ክርስቲያኑ ፡ ለመርቆርዮስ፤ ፡ ሰማዕት ፡ በምስር ። ወተርኅወ ፡ "ሎቱ ፡ ፕኅት፤ ፡ ባሕቲቱ!፤ ፡ ወቦአ፤ ፡ እንዘ ፣ ይጹዓን ፡ ምስሌሁ ፡ ዲበ ፡ ፈረስ ፤ ወአውረዶ ፡ ኀበ ፡ መንጦላዕተ ፡ ምኩራብ! ወተሐብአ ፡ እምኔሁ። ወኮነ ፡ ውእቱ ፡ ወሬዛ ፡ ይቀውም ፡ ውስተ፣ቤተ፣ ክርስቲያን ፡ እንዘ ፣ ይደነግፅ ። ወየሐሊ፤ ፡ በልቡ ፡ ወይቤ ፡ ቦኑ ፡ ዛቲ ፡ ቤተ ፡ ክርስቲያኖሙ ፡ ለክርስቲያን ። ወኮነ ፣ ይሬእዮሙ ፣ ለመኃትው ፡ እንዘ ፡ ይበርሁ ፡ እምሥዕላት፤” ፡ ወኮነ ፡ ዝንቱ ፡ በኀቤሁ ፡ ከመ ፡ ሕልም ። ወይቤ ፡ አኮኑ ፡ ይእዜ ፡ ነበርኩ ፤ ውስተ ፡ ገዳም ። ወቆመ ፡ ውስተ ፡፤ ምኩራብ ፡ እስከ ፡ ቀርበ" ፡ ጽባሕ ። ወቦአ ፡ መጋቤ ፡ ቤተ ፡ ክርስቲያን ፡ ወረከቦ ፡ እንዘ ፣ ይቀውም ፡ ኀበ ፡ ምኩራብ ፤ ወመሰሎ ፡ ከመ ፡ ውአቱ ፡ ፈያታዊ ፡ ወፈቀደ ፡ መጋቢ?፤ ፡ ከመ ፡ ይክልሕ ። ወአመረ ፡ በእዴሁ ፡ ወይቤ'" ፡ አርምም ፡ ነዓ ፡ ኀቤየ ። ወቀርበ ፡ ኀቤሁ ፡ መጋቢ ፡ ወይቤሎ ፡ ወሬዛ ፡ምንት ፡ ውእቱ ፡ ዝመካን ። ወይቤሎ ፡ ዛቲ ፡ ቤተ ፡ ክርስቲያን ፡ 'ዘመርቆርዮስ ፡ ሰማዕት ፡ በምስር ። ወይቤሎ ፡ ወሬዛ ፡ ወዛቲኑ" ፡ ሀገር ፡ ምስር ፡ ወይቤሎ ፡ እወ ። ወይቤሎ ፡ መጋቢ ፡ አንተሰ፤፤ ፡ ከመ ፡ ዘጠፍአ ፡ ልብከ፤፡‹ ፣ ንግረኒ" ፡ ዘበጽሐከ ። ወሶቤሃ ፡ ገብአ ፡ ልቡ ፡ ወይቤሎ ፡ እፎ' ፡ ዘ[ኢ]ጠፍአ ፡ ልብየ ፡ ወአንሰ፤ ፡ ሀለውኩ ፡ በዛቲ ፣ ሌሊት ፡ በገዳመ ፡ እገሌ ፡ ወኢየእመርኩ ፡ እፎ ፡ ዘበጻሕኩ ፡ ዝየ ፡ አላ ፡ እግዚአብሔር ፡ ያአምር ፡ ዘንተ ። ወሶበ ፡ ሰምዓ ፡ መጋቢ ፡ ስማ፣ ለይእቲ ፡ ገዳም ፡ አንከረ ፣ ወይቤሎ ፡ አኮኑ ፡ እቤለከ ፡ በአማን ፡ 'ከመ ፡ ልብከ ጠፍአ፤፤ ፡ ወኢታአምር ፡ ዘትቤ ። እስመ ፡ መጠኑ ፡ ለውእቱ ፡ መካን” ፡ እስከ ፡ ዝየ፡!፤ ፡ ፳ወ፪መዓልት ። ወበአማን ፡ አንተ ፡ ፈያታዊ ፡ ወኀይሉ ፡ 'ለሰማዕት ፡፣ መርቆርዮስ፤፤ ፡ አኃዘከ፡፤ ፡ እስመ ፡ ዝንቱ ፡ ቅዱስ ፡ መርቆርዮስ፤ ፡ ሰማዕት ፡ ብእሲ፤ ፡ዘኃደገ ፡ ክብረ ፡ ዝንቱ ፡ ዓለም፤፤ ። ወኩነንዎ ፡ ከሓዲያን ፡ዓቢየ ፡ኩነኔ፣ በእንተ ፡ ስሙ ፡ ለክርስቶስ ፡ 'ወሞተ ፡ ሰማዕት፤፤ ፡ ወተወከፎ ፡ እግዚአብሔር ፡ ውስተ ፡ መንግሥቱ ። ወተሐንጸ ፡ ሎቱ ፡ አብያተ ፡ ክርስቲያናት ፡ በስሙ ፡ ውስተ ፡ ኩሉ ፡ መካናት ፤ ወይሴብሕዎ"፡ቦ ፡ ለእግዚአብሔር ፤ ውስቴቶሙ፤፤ ፡ ወሎቱ ፡ ትንብልና ፡ "ወኩሎሙ ፡ ሰብእ ፡ ይነብቡ ፡ ቦቱ፤፤ ፣ ወይከሥት ፡ ተኣምራተ ። ወይ ቤሎ ፡ ወሬዛ ፡ እፎ ፡ ኮነ ፡ 'አምሳለ ፡ አርአያሁ፤ ። ወይቤሎ ፡ መጋቢ ፡ ውእቱሰ ፡
golden belt. He stood before him and said to him: "Where are you from and how are you lost alone in this desert?" He said to him: "I got down (from the camel) for a physical need and (my companions) left me and went away." (The young man) said: "Come, mount behind me on this horse." He mounted behind him and the horse flew with him (= the young man) into the air and brought him quickly to the church of Margoryos the martyr in Mesr. The door opened for him alone, he entered, being mounted on horseback with him (= the Muslim), made him get down near the veil of the choir, and hid himself from his (sight). The young man stood in the church trembling. || thought in his heart and said: "Is this a church of the Christians?" || saw the lamps that were shining from the images; that was to him like a dream. He said: "Do I not find myself in the desert?" And he stood in the choir until the morning approached. The sacristan of the church entered and found him standing near the choir; it seemed to the sacristan that it was a thief and he wanted to shout. But (the young man) made a sign with his hand and said to him: "Be silent, come to me." The sacristan approached him and the young man said to him: "What is this place?" (The sacristan) said to him: "It is the church of Margoryos the martyr in Mesr." The young man said to him: "Is this city Mesr?" (The sacristan) said to him: "Yes." The sacristan said to him: "You are as if your spirit were extinguished. Tell me what happened to you." Then his (= the young man's) spirit returned and he said to him: "How would my spirit not have been extinguished, while I was this night in such a desert and I do not know how I arrived here? But God knows that." When the sacristan learned the name of this desert, he was astonished and said to him: "Did I not tell you with truth that your spirit is extinguished and (that) you do not know what you are saying? Indeed, the journey from that place to here (is) twenty-two days. In truth, you are a thief and the power of the martyr Margoryos has seized you, for this saint Margoryos the martyr (is) a man who abandoned the glory of this world. The infidels made him undergo great torture because of the name of Christ; he died a martyr and God welcomed him into his kingdom. Churches have been built for him in his name in every place, God is praised there; to him (= Margoryos) (is) the intercession, all men speak of him and he manifests miracles." The young man said to him: "What does his appearance look like?" The sacristan said to him: "He looks like you." Then he uncovered his (= the saint's) image. When the young man saw his image, he said: "In truth, it is he who appeared to me, to me, in the desert! He carried me on his horse and brought me here; here is his golden belt with which I saw him girded." He said to him (= the sacristan): "Here I tell you that I am a Muslim of this city; the name of my father (is) [Name], the judge. This miracle is enough for me to become a Christian. Now, hide me in a place, do not reveal what concerns me to anyone, bring me an old priest so that he may teach me the way of God and strengthen me in the right faith." The sacristan placed him in a place alone and brought him a priest. (The latter) baptized him with Christian baptism and brought him the writings of the Church and

። "ወኮነት ፡እሙ ፡ትበኪ። ፡ ላዕሌሁ ፡ ሌሊተ ፡ ወመዓልተ ፡ እምብገዝኃ ፡ ብካያ፤ ፡ ሰደድዎ፤ ፡ ወሖረ ፡ ኅበ ፡ ገዳመ ፡ አስቄጥስ ። ወነበረ ፡ ውስተ ፡ ደብረ ፤ ፡ አባ ፡ መቃርስ ፡፤ መጠነ ፡ ፪ዓመት ፡ እንዘ ፡ ይትጋደል ፡ ወይጸመድ?፤ ። ወእምድኅረዝ ፡ ይቤሎ ፡ 8መነኮስ ፡ ኢየበቀዓከ ፡ ዝንቱ፤” ፡ ዘእንበለ ፡ ትሖር ፡ ኅበ ፡ ምስር ፡ ወታግ ህድ ፡ሃይማኖተከ ። ወተንሥአ ፡ ወሖረ ፡ ኅበ ፡ ምስር ፡ወአኀዝዎ ፡ አቡሁ ፡ ወአኅኀዊሁ ፤ ወወሰድዎ፤፤ ፡ ኅበ ፡ ንጉሥ ፡ ሐክም፤፤ ፡ ዘይሰመይ ፡ መኩንን ። ወይቤልዎ ፡ ዝንቱ ፡ ውእቱ ፡ ወልድነ ፡ ወኀደገ ፡ 'ሃይማኖተ ፡ ተንበላት፤ ፡ ወቦአ ፡ ውስተ ፡ ሃይማኖት፣፤ ክርስቲያን ። ወይቤሎ ፡ መኩንን ፡ እወኑ" ፡ዛዘይቤሉ ፡ በእንቲአከ ። ወነገሮ ፡፣ ለመኩንን ፡ ዘኮነ ፡ እምኔሁ ፡ በውስተ ፡ ገዳም ፡ 'ፍኖተ ፡ መካ" ፡ ወዘከመ ፡ አስተ
the holy Gospel, and (the young man) remained thus. A month later arrived the Muslims who had gone to Makkä; all the Muslims went out to welcome their relatives. The judge came to meet his friend; his friend shed bitter tears and told him how his son had been delayed in the desert and how they had left him and had arrived. When his father heard that, he tore his clothes and, likewise, his brothers and his sons tore their clothes, and they remained lamenting (?) days. After that, a Muslim saw the young man who had become a Christian coming out of the church of the saint Margoryos. The Muslim went, told (it) to his father and his brothers, and said to them: "If your son had not died in the desert on the road to Makkä, I would say that it is the one I saw on this day near the church of Margoryos dressed in monastic clothes; in truth, he looks like your son." When his father and his brothers heard that, they stood up, went, found him, seized him, and said to him: "What have you done? You have covered us with shame among the judges of the Muslims!" He said to them: "I, I am a Christian and I believe in my Lord Jesus Christ, Son of the living God." When he had said that, they made him undergo great torture and threw him into a dark pit. || remained there seven days and seven nights without food or drink while (the people) poured their urine and the filth they swept out of their house on him. His mother wept over him night and day; because of the abundance of her tears, they chased him away and he went to the desert of 'Asqêtes (= Scetis). He remained in the monastery of Abbä Magäres (= Macarius) for two years devoting himself to (spiritual) combat and asceticism. After that, a monk said to him: "This will not be profitable for you without you going to Mesr and proclaiming your faith." (The young man) stood up and went to Mesr. His father and his brothers seized him and led him before the king Hakem who is called "governor". They said to him: "This is our son, he has abandoned the faith of the Muslims and has entered into the Christian faith." The governor said to him: "Is it true, what they say about you?" || told the governor what had happened to him in the desert on the road to Makkä, how the saint Margoryos had appeared to him, how he had made him mount with him on his horse, had brought him in the blink of an eye, and had made him enter his church which

ይቤሎ ፡ 8መነኮስ ፡ ኢየበቀዓከ ፡ ዝንቱ፤” ፡ ዘእንበለ ፡ ትሖር ፡ ኅበ ፡ ምስር ፡ ወታግ ህድ ፡ሃይማኖተከ ። ወተንሥአ ፡ ወሖረ ፡ ኅበ ፡ ምስር ፡ወአኀዝዎ ፡ አቡሁ ፡ ወአኅኀዊሁ ፤ ወወሰድዎ፤፤ ፡ ኅበ ፡ ንጉሥ ፡ ሐክም፤፤ ፡ ዘይሰመይ ፡ መኩንን ። ወይቤልዎ ፡ ዝንቱ ፡ ውእቱ ፡ ወልድነ ፡ ወኀደገ ፡ 'ሃይማኖተ ፡ ተንበላት፤ ፡ ወቦአ ፡ ውስተ ፡ ሃይማኖት፣፤ ክርስቲያን ። ወይቤሎ ፡ መኩንን ፡ እወኑ" ፡ዛዘይቤሉ ፡ በእንቲአከ ። ወነገሮ ፡፣ ለመኩንን ፡ ዘኮነ ፡ እምኔሁ ፡ በውስተ ፡ ገዳም ፡ 'ፍኖተ ፡ መካ" ፡ ወዘከመ ፡ አስተ

ርአየ ፡ ሎቱ ፡ ቅዱስ ፡ መርቆርዮስ ፡ ወዘከመ ፡ አጽዓኖ ፡ ምስሌሁ ፡ ዲበ ፡ ፈረሱ ፡ ወአብጽሖቶ ፡ ከመ ፡ ቅጽበት ፡ ዓይን ፡ ወአብኦ ፡ ውስተ ፡ ቤተ ፡ ክርስቲያነ ፡ ዚአሁ ፡ ዘበምስር ፡ ወዘከመ ፡ ርእየ ፡ ሥዕሎ ፡ በውስተ ፡ ቤተ ፡ ክርስቲያኑ ፡ ወአጠየቆ" ፡ ከመ ፡ ውእቱ ፡ ዘአስተርአዮ ፡ ሎቱ ፡ በውስተ ፡ ገዳም ። ወሶበ ፡ ሰምዓ ፡ ንጉሥ ፡ ሐክም ፡ ዘንተ ፡ ነገረ ፡ አንከረ ፡ ፈድፋደ ፡ ወፈርሃ ፡ ዓቢየ ፡ ፍርሃተ ፡ ወይቤሎ ፡ ለውእቱ ፡ ወሬዛ ፡ ምንተ ፡ ትፈቅድ ። ወይቤሎ ፡ እስእለከ ፡ ከመ ፡ ተአዝዝ ፡ ሊተ ፡ ከመ ፡ እሕንጽ ፡ ቤተ ፡ ክርስቲያነ ፡ በስሙ ፡ ለቅዱስ ፡ መርቆርዮስ ፡ ሰማዕት ። ወአዘዘ ፡ ሎቱ ፡ ንጉሥ ፡ ከመ ፡ ይሕንጽ ፡ ቤተ ፡ ክርስቲያነ ፡ ወሐነጸ ፡ ቤተ ፡ ክርስቲያነ"" ፡ በስሙ ፡ 'ለቀዱስ ፣ መርቆርዮስ፥?፤።?። ፡፣ ወኮነ ፤ ውስቴታ ፣ ተኣም ራት ፡ ወመንክራት ፡ ዓበይት ። ወደረሰ ፡ ፪መጻሕፍተ ፡ በጸጋ፤ ፡ መንፈስ ፡ ቅዱስ ፡ በእንተ ፡ ትርጓሜ ፤ ሃይማኖት ፡ ርትዕት ፡ ወሞኦሙ ፡ ለዓላዊያን ፣ ወአረማዊያን ፡ ወተንበላት ። ወአሥመሮ ፡ ለእግዚአብሔር ፡ ወአዕረፈ ፡ በሰላም፤ ። ወስሙሰ ፥ ለውእቱ ፡ ወሬዛ ፡ ዘሰመይዎ ፡ በጊዜ ፡ አጥመቅዎ ፡ ዮሐንስ ። ወዝንቱ ፡ ኩሉ ፡ ኮነ ፡ በጸሎቱ ፡ ለቅዱስ ፡ ወብዙኃት ፡ ተኣምራቲሁ ፡ 'ለብፁዕ ፡ ወቅዱስ ፡ መርቆር
(is) in Mesr, how he had seen his image in his church and (how the saint) had made him understand that it was he who had appeared to him in the desert. When the king Hakem heard this speech, he was very astonished, felt great fear, and said to the young man: "What do you want?" (The young man) said to him: "I ask you to give me a mandate to build a church in the name of the saint Margoryos the martyr." The king gave him a mandate to build a church; he built a church in the name of the saint Margoryos and great miracles and wonders happened there. He composed two books by the grace of the Holy Spirit on the interpretation of the right faith; he defeated (in controversy) the infidels, the pagans, and the Muslims. He pleased God and died in peace. As for the name of this young man by which he was named at the moment when he was baptized, (it is) Yohannes (= John). All this took place thanks to the prayer of the saint, and numerous (are) the miracles of the blessed and holy Margoryos. May his prayer, his blessing, and his intercession be with us and with all the children of baptism forever, amen.
ዮስ፤ ። ጸሎቱ ፡ ወበረከቱ ፡ ወትንብልናሁ ፡ የሀሉ ፡ ምስሌነ ፡ ወምስለ ፡ ኩሎሙ ፡ ውሉደ ፡ ጥምቀት ፡ ለዓለም ፡ አሜን ። ወበዛቲ ፡ዕለት ፡ ካዕበ ፣፡ ተዝካሮሙ ፡ ለአቃሮስ ፡ ወሮማኖስ ። በረከቶሙ ፡ ተሀሉ፣ ምስሌነ ፡ ለዓለመ ፡ ዓለም ።

[On this day also died Sebla Wangël, queen of the faith, mistress of actions, (whose) memory is beautiful, the wife of the king Lebna Dengel. May her blessing be with our king 'Iyäsu and with his dear Za-Manfas Qeddus in the ages of ages]
አመ ፡ ፳5ለኅዳር ፡ ምንባብ ። በስመ ፡ አብ ፡ ወወልድ ፡ ወመንፈስ ፡ ቅዱስ ፡ አሐዱ ፡ አምላክ ። በዛቲ ፡ ዕለት ፡ ኮነ ፡ ቅዱስ ፡ በላርያኖስ፤ ። ኮነ ፡ እምሰብአ ፡ ሀገረ ፡ ሮሜ ፡ ወኮኑ ፡ አበዊሁ ፡ ከሓዲያነ ። ወአስተዋሰብዎ ፡ 'ለወለተ ፡ ፳ብእሲ ፡ እምልሂቃነ ፡ ሀገረ ፤ ሮሜ ፡ ወስማ ፡ ለይእቲ ፡ ወለት ፤ ኪሊኪያቶ ፡ ወክርስቲያናዊት ፡ ይእቲ ፡ ወታመልኮ ፡ ለእግዚእነ" ፡ ክርስቶስ ። ወሶበ ፡ ቦአ ፡ ምስሌሃ ፡ ውስተ ፡ ጽርሑ ፡ አፍቀራ ፡ ፈድፋደ ።
[READING] FOR THE 26TH OF HEDAR In the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit, one God. On this day there is the saint Baläryänos (= Valerian). He was from the people of the city of Rome and his parents were infidels. They married him to the daughter of a man among the notables of the city of Rome; the name of this young girl (was) Kilikiyä (= Cecilia), she was a Christian and rendered worship to
"ወሶበ ፡ አእመረት ፡ ከመ ፡ ውእቱ ፡ አፍቀራ ፡ ፈድፋደ ፡ አኃዘት ፡ ትከሥት ፡ ሎቱ ፡ ሃይማኖተ ፡ ክርስቶስ ፡ ወአምነ ፡ በእዴሃ ፡ ወተጠምቀ ፡ ጥምቀተ ፡ ክርስትና ። ወሶበ ፡ በርሃ ፡ በጸጋ ፡ መለኮታዊት ፡ ወመሀራ ፡ ለእኅቱ ፡ ተትቡስያ ፡ ወአምነት"' ፡ ወተጠምቀት ፡ ጥምቀተ ፡ ክርስትና ። ወቅዱስሰ ፡ በላርያኖስ ፡ ተጋደለ ፤ ተጋድሎ ፤ ዓቢየ ፡ እስከ ፡ ኮኑ ፡ መላእክት ፡ ይመጽኡ ፡ ኅቤሁ ፡ ኩሎ ፡ ጊዜ ፡ ወይከሥቱ ፡ ሎቱ ፡ ዘይፈቅድ፤፤ ፡ ወይምህርዎ ፡ ምሥጢራተ ፡ ዘይከውን ፡ እምቅደመ ፡ ይኩን ። ወሶበ ፡ ነግሠ ፡ ዲዮቅልጥያኖስ ፡ ከሓዲ ፤ አመንደቦሙ ፡ ለሕዝበ ፡ ክርስቲያን ፣፤ ወኩነኖሙ ፡ ወቀተሎሙ ፡ ለብዙኃን ፡ እምኔሆሙ ፡ ወኮኑ ፡ ሰማዕተ ። 'ወኮነ ፡ ዝንቱ ፡ ቅዱስ" ፡ ወእኅቱ ፡ የዓውዱ!" ፡ ወይነሥኡ ፡ አብድንቲሆሙ ፡ ለሰማዕታት ፡ ወይገንዝዎሙ ፡፣ ወይቀብርዎሙ ። ወሶበ ፡ አእመረ ፤ ቦሙ ፡ አሐዱ ፡ ብእሲ ፡ እኩይ፤ ፡ አስተዋደ ዮሙ ፡ኅበ ፡ ጦስዮስ!" ፡ ዓርኩ ፡ ለንጉሥ ። ወአዘዘ ፡ ከመ ፡ ያብጽሕዎሙ!?” ፡ ወሶበ ፡ በጽሑ ፤ ቆሙ፤ ፡ ቅድሜሁ ፡ ወተስእሎሙ ፡ በእንተ ፡ ሃይማኖቶሙ ። ወተአምኑ ፡ ከመ ፡ እሙንቱ ፡ ክርስቲያን ፡ ወኢክሕዱ ። ወተካየዶሙ፤” ፡ ኪዳናተ ፡ ብዙኃተ?' ፡ ለእመ፤ ፡ ከሐድዎ፤" ፡ ለእግዚእነ ፡ ክርስቶስ ፡ ወይሥሠዑ ፡ ለጣዖታት ። ወኢሰምዑ ፡ ትእዛዞ ፡ ወኢፈቀዱ ፡ ኪዳናቲሁ ። ወአፍርሆሙ ፡ ወይቤሎሙ ፡ ለአመ ፡'ኢሰማዕክሙ ፡ ትእዛዝየ፤ ፡ ወኢሦዕክሙ” ፡ ለአማልክትየ ፡ አንሰ ፡ እቤንነክሙ ፡ በመክፈልተ ፡ ኩነኔ ፡ ብዙኅ ፡ ወኢፈርሁ ፤ እምኩነኔሁ ፡ ግሙራ ። ወሶበ ፡ ርእየ ፡ መኩንን ፡ ጽን ዖሙ ፡ ወትዕግሥቶሙ ፡ መጠዎሙም ፡ ለዘ ፡ ይመትር ፡ ክሳዶሙ ። ወሶበ ፡ መተሩ ፡ ርእሶ[ሙ]” ፡ ርእይዎሙ ፡ እለ ፡ ነበሩ ፡ በህየ ፡ ለመላእክት ፡ ብሩሃን ፡ እንዘ ፡ ይትቀበሉ ፡ ነፍሶሙ ፡ በዓቢይ ፤ ፍሥሓ ። ወሶበ ፡ ርእየ ፡ መኩንን ፡ ዘንተ ፡ አምነ ፡ በእግዚእነ ፡ ክርስቶስ ። ወንጉሥሰ ፡ ሞቅሖ፥ ፡ ለመኩንን ፡ ይመዓልተ ፡ ወበራብዕ ፡ ዕለት ፡ አውፅኦ ፡ እምቤተ ፡ ሞቅሕ ፡ ወአዘዘ ፡ ከመ ፡ ይምትሩ ፡ ርእሶ፤ ። 'ወመ ተሩ ፡ ርእሶ። ፡ በሰይፍ ፡ ወነሥአ ፡ አክሊለ ፡ ስምዕ ፡ በመንግሥተ ፡ ሰማያት ፡ምስለ፣ ቅዱስ ፡ በላርያኖስ ፡ ወብእሲቱ ፤ ኪሊኪያ ። ጸሎቶሙ ፡ ወበረከቶሙ ፡ የሀሉ ፡ ምስሌነ ፡ አሜን ።
our Lord Christ. When (Baläryänos) entered with her into their bridal chamber, he loved her very much. When she knew that he loved her very much, she undertook to reveal to him the faith of Christ, he believed through her intermediary and was baptized with Christian baptism. When he was illuminated by divine grace, he instructed his sister Tatbusyä, she believed and was baptized with Christian baptism. The saint Baläryänos led a great (spiritual) combat to such a point that the angels would come to him at any moment, reveal to him what he wanted, and instruct him in the mysteries that were to happen before they had happened. When Diyoqletyänos (= Diocletian) the impious reigned, he persecuted the Christians, tormented them, and (had) many of them killed, and they were martyrs. This saint and his sister would circulate, take the corpses of the martyrs, shroud them, and bury them. As a wicked man had learned (this) about them, he denounced them to Tosyos (= Tarsius), the friend of the emperor. (The latter) ordered them to be brought; when they had arrived, they stood before him and he questioned them about their faith. They confessed that they were Christians and did not deny (it). He made them many promises (that he would fulfill) if they denied our Lord Christ and sacrificed to the idols. They did not obey his order and did not want his promises. He (wanted) to frighten them and said to them: "If you do not obey my order and if you do not sacrifice to my gods, I will (have) you tortured with many kinds of torture." They were not afraid of his torture at all. When the governor saw their strength and their constancy, he handed them over to the one who would cut their necks. When their heads had been cut off, those who were there saw luminous angels welcoming their souls with great joy. When the governor saw that, he believed in our Lord Christ. The emperor imprisoned the governor for three days and, on the fourth day, he had him taken out of the prison and ordered his head to be cut off. They cut off his head with the sword and he received the crown of martyrdom in the kingdom of heaven with the saint Baläryänos and his wife Kilikiyä. May their prayer and their blessing be with us, amen.


[p. 374] {#po.44.p374}
ወበዛቲ ፡ ዕለት ፡ ካዕበ ፡ ተዝካሩ ፡ ለቅዱስ ፡ ጎርጎርዮስ ፡ ዘደሴትቶ ። ጸሎቱ ፡ ወበረከቱ ፡ የሀሉ ፡ ምስሌነ ፡ ለዓለም ፡ አሜን ።
On this day also (there is) the commemoration of the saint Gorgoryos (= Gregory) of Nyssa. May his prayer and his blessing be with us forever, amen.
ወበዛቲ ፡ ዕለት ፡ ካዕበ ፡ ስምዓ ፡ ኮኑ ፡ ሰማዕታተ ፡ ናግራን ፣ ወቅዱስ ፡ ቲሩት ፡ አቡሆሙ ፡ በሃምስ ፡ አመተ ፡ መንግሥቱ ፡ ለዮስጢኖስ ፡ ንጉሥ ፡እንዘ ፡ ሊቀ ፡ ጳጳሳት ፡ ዘኢየሩሳሌም ፡ አባ ፡ ዮሐንስ ፡ ወበቀጐስጥንጥንያ ፡ ጢሞቴዎስ?" ፡ ወበእለ ፡ እስክንድ ርያ ፤ ጢሞቴዎስ ፡ ወበአንጾኪያ ፣፤ ኤዎፍራንዮስ ፡ ወንጉሠ ፡ ኢትዮጵያ ፡ ካሌብ ፤

፡ ሐሰ ፡ ለነ ፡ ኢንገብር ፡ ዘንተ ፡ ግብረ ፣ ወኢንክህዶ ፡ ለክርስቶስ ፡ አምላክነ ፡ ዘአመነ ፡ ቦቱ ፡ ወተጠመቅነ ፡ በስሙ ። ወሰሚዖ ፡ አይሁዳዊ ፡ ርኩስ ፡ ቀተለ ፤ እደ ፡ ወአንስተ ፡፤ ደቂቀ ፡ ወወራዙተ ፡ ሕፃናተ ፡ ወአዕራገ ፡ መጠነ ፤ ጓ፻ወ፪፻8ወ፪ነፍስ ። ወለእለ ፡ ተርፋሂ ፡ አንስት ፡ አዘዘ ፡ ይምትሩ ፡ ክሳውዲሆን ፡ በሰይፍ ፡ ወኮነ ፡ ጐልቆን ፡ ፪፻፳ ወ፪ ነፍስ ። ወእምዝ ፡ አኃዛ ፡ ለቅድስት ፡ ድማሃ ፡ ወለተ ፡ ራብዕ ፡ ብእሲቱ ፡ ለቅዱስ ፡ ቲሩት ፡ ወይእቲሰ ፡ ብእሲት ፡ ሠናይተ ፡ ላህይ ፡
Yohannes (= John), Timotëwos (= Timothy) (being patriarch) in Constantinople, Timotëwos in Alexandria, and 'Éwofränyos (= Euphranius) in 'Ansokiyä (= Antioch), the king of Ethiopia (being) Kalëb (= Caleb) the righteous. In that time reigned over the land of Säbä (= Saba) a Jew whose name (was) Finhas (= Phineas); (he was) an impious infidel and a shedder of the blood of men. Formerly the land of Säbä remained under the authority of Ethiopia. When 'Asbäsyänos (= Vespasian) and Titos (= Titus), the emperors of Rome, chased the Jews, they seized it (= the land of Säbä) subsequently. Next to the land of Säbä, there was a very large city; many people dwelt there, faithful and (people) believing in Jesus Christ. The king of the Jews arrived there to destroy the holy city, friend of our Lord Jesus Christ, and plunder its churches. Arrived at this city, he saw its wall which (was) surrounded by signs of the cross, many men of war standing on its gates and its rampart. The king cursed by the Jews became angry and wanted to enter the city. He could not by ruse, because the power of God reinforced it. But he killed those who were working the land whom he met outside. As for the children, he took them prisoner and made them slaves. This Jew was similar to the devil, the Adversary; he sent (a message) to them (= the inhabitants) swearing by the name of the Lord, the God of the Torah and the prophets, and he said to them: "I do not want combat with you, nor bad treatment against any of the people of the city, nor that a (single) drop of blood be shed there, but I want to see the constructions of the city, its squares, and its markets." The people of the city believed in his word. Then Hirut, son of Kä'eb, said to them: "Do not believe in the word of this infidel Jew, for he is an impious man; do not open to him." But (the inhabitants) refused to listen to his word and opened the gates to him. The Jew, (once) entered, ordered first to plunder the goods of the people of the city and to light a fire in such a way that its height would rise to the sky. He ordered Abbä Päwlos (= Paul), the bishop of the city, to be brought. When they told him that he was dead, he (had) his bones taken out of the tomb and (had them) burned in the fire. Then he gathered the priests, the deacons, the monks, the widows, and the orphans who watched day and night reading the holy Scriptures and, them too, he (had them) thrown into the fire. Their number was four hundred and twenty-seven souls. || wanted to frighten the Christians by that. Then he ordered a yoke to be put on the neck of the saint Hirut and to chain his hands and his feet; he imprisoned (also) all the notables and the chiefs of the city. || ordered a herald to circulate in the city saying: "Whoever will not deny Christ will die a bad death." When the people, holy Christians, heard (that), they cried out saying: "Far from us (that)! We will not do this thing and we will not deny Christ our God in whom we believe and in whose name we have been baptized." Hearing (that), the Jew

ንሖር ፤ ፍጡነ ፤ ውስተ ፡ ሕይወት ፡ ዘለዓለም ፡ እስመ ፡ ኢንሬእያ ፡ ለዛቲ ፡ እሳት ፡ ዘእንበለ ፡ ዮም ። ወለሊሃ ፡ ተወርወት ፡ ውስተ ፡ እሳት ፡ ምስለ ፡ ወልዳ ። ወርእዮሙ ፡ ሕዝበ ፡ ክርስቲያን ፡ ተባደሩ ፡ መንፈቆሙ ፡ ውስተ ፡ እሳት ፤ ወመንፈቆሙ ፡ ውስተ ፤ አፈ ፡ መጥባሕት ፡ እስከ ፡ አስተዓፀቡ ፡ ኩሎሙ ፡ ማኅበረ ፡ አይሁድ ። ወእምዝ ፡ አስተርአየ ፡ እሳት ፡ በውስተ ፡ ሰማይ ፡ ምሉአ ፡ ከዊኖ ፡ ጓመዓልተ ፡ ወጓሌሊተ ።
impure (had) men and women, children and adolescents, infants and old men killed: four thousand two hundred and fifty-two souls. As for the women who remained, he ordered their heads to be cut off with the sword. Their number was two hundred and twenty-seven souls. Then, he (had) the saint Demähä, daughter of Räb'e, the wife of the saint Hirut - she was a woman of very great beauty -, and her two daughters with her seized; the sun had never touched them except when they entered the veranda of their house. (Once) arrived, they stood before the king; the king made them promises with much cunning so that they would deny their faith. As they refused (it) to him, he ordered their veils to be removed so that the women of the city wept over them. One of his (= Demähä's) daughters, the one who was the youngest - who was twelve years old -, spat in the king's face. When he saw (that), a soldier drew his sword and cut off her neck and the neck of her sister. Then the impious Jew ordered the saint Demähä to be made to drink the blood of her daughters. Having tasted (it), she said: "I give you thanks, my Lord Christ, Son of the living God, because you have made your servant taste the blood of the sacrifice of the offering of her daughters." They seized her too and (the king) ordered her head to be cut off with the sword. They finished their martyrdom there by the sword. (The king) ordered the saint Hirut and those who (were) with him to be brought from the prison; their number (was) three hundred and fifty men. The Jew (wanted) to force the saint Hirut and those who (were) with him to deny their faith. The saint Hirut said: "I have remained seventy-eight years worshipping God and I have arrived until the fourth generation; today, I will rejoice when I become a martyr and when I die for his holy name. I, I told my people not to believe in your oath and that you were a liar, but they did not believe my word; assuredly the will of God has brought me into this combat (for the faith)." Upon hearing (that), the king of the Jews became very angry and ordered him to be taken into the valley and, there, to have his neck cut off with the sword. Hearing (this order), the saint Hirut prayed to God to strengthen the empire of Rome and the empire of Ethiopia and to destroy the kingdom of the impious Jew. He blessed his people and exchanged greetings with them, then the soldiers cut off the heads of the saints. There was a Christian woman there, she had a five-year-old son; she took some of the saints' blood, anointed herself (with it), and anointed her son (with it). Seeing (that), the soldiers led her, (having) tied (her), to the king, and she was thrown into the fire; as for her son, the Jews took him to them and (the king) said to him: "Is it I whom you love or the one who is called Christ?" The child said: "I, I love Christ, for I am his servant; now, let me go to my mother." As (the king) held him, he bit the king's foot and, having freed himself, entered into the fire, and he became a martyr. They also brought a woman carrying a ten-month-old infant. Her mother said to her: "I cannot spare you this fire." The child said to her: "My mother, let us go quickly into eternal life, for we will only see this fire today." (The woman) threw herself into the fire with her son. Seeing (that), the Christians ran, half

ወሐዊሮ ፡ ንጉሠ ፡ አይሁድ ፡ ውስተ ፡ ሀገሩ ፡ ለአከ ፡ ኀበ ፡ ኩሎሙ ፡ ነገሥታት ፡እንዘ፣ ይትሜካሕ ፡ በኃይሉ ። ወሰሚዖ ፡ ዮስጢኖስ ፡ ንጉሠ ፡ ሮሜ ፡ ለአከ ፡ ኅበ ፡ ጢሞቴዎስ ፡ ሊቀ ፡ ጳጳሳት ፡ ዘእለ ፡ እስክንድርያ ፡ ከመ ፡ ይፈኑ ፡ መጽሐፈ ፡ መልእክት ፡ ወመሐላ ፡ ኅበ ፡ ካሌብ ፡ ንጉሠ ፡ ኢትዮጵያ ፡ ከመ ፡ ይ[ፅ]ብኦ ፤ ለውእቱ ፡ አይሁዳዊ ፡ ወከመ ፡ ይትበቀል ፡ ደሞሙ ፡ ለሰብአ ፡ ናግራን ። "ወለአከ ፡ አባ ፡ ጢሞቴዎስ ፡ ኅበ ፡ ካሌብ ፡ ንጉሠ ፡ ኢትዮጵያ” ። ወሰሚዖ ፡ ካሌብ ፡ ንጉሠ ፡ ኢትዮጵያ ፡ ሖረ ፡ ውስተ ፡ ሀገሩ ፡ ምስለ ፡ ብዙኅ ፡ ሰራዊቱ?" ፡ ወብዙኅ ፡ አሕማር ፡ እምድኅረ ፡ ነሥአ ፡ በረከቶ ፡ ለአባ ፡ ጳንጠሌዎን ፡ ዘጾማዕት ። ወበጺሖ ፡ ሀየ ፡ አጥፍአ ፡ ኩሎ ፡ ሀገሮ ፡ ለንጉሠ ፡ ሳባ ፡ ወኢያትረፈ ፡ ምንተኒ ፡ ኢሰብአ ፡ ወኢእንስሳ ፡ ወሐደሰ ፡ ሕንጻሐ ፡ ለሀገረ ፡ ናግራን ፡ ወዓቀመ ፡ ተዝካረ ፡ ሰማዕታቲሃ ። ወፈነወ ፡ ብስራተ ፡ ኀበ ፡ ጢሞቴዎስ ፡ ሊቀ ፡ ጳጳ ሳት ፡ ወዮስጢኖስ ፡ ንጉሠ ፡ ሮሜ ፡ ወኮነ ፡ ዐቢይ ፡ ፍሥሐ ፡ ወአእኩትዎ ፡ ለክርስቶስ ። በረከቶሙ ፡ ተሀሉ ፡ምስለ ፡ ንጉሥነ ፡ ኢያሱ ፡ ወምስለ ፡ ፍቁሩ ፡ ዘመንፈስ ፡ ቅዱስ ፡ ለዓለመ ፡ ዓለም ።
of them into the fire and the other half to the point of the swords so that the whole troop of the Jews found (that) painful. Then there appeared in the sky an immense fire for forty days and forty nights. Having gone to his city, the king of the Jews sent (messages) to all the kings boasting of his power. Upon learning (that), Yostinos, the emperor of Rome, dispatched to Timotëwos, patriarch of Alexandria, so that (the latter) might send a letter and a sworn treaty to Kälëb, king of Ethiopia, so that (the latter) might make war on the Jew and so that he might avenge the blood of the people of Nägrän. Abbä Timotëwos sent (a message) to Kälëb, king of Ethiopia. Learning (that), Kälëb, king of Ethiopia, went to his (= the Jewish king's) land with many of his soldiers and many ships after having received the blessing of Abbä Pantalëwon of the cell. Arrived there, he destroyed the whole land of the king of Säbä, left nothing remaining, neither men nor animals, rebuilt the city of Nägrän, and established the commemoration of its martyrs. He sent (this) good news to Timotëwos the patriarch and (to) Yostinos, the emperor of Rome; there was great joy and they gave thanks to Christ. May their blessing be with our king 'Iyäsu and with his dear Za-Manfas Qeddus
=<ወበዛቲ፣፤ዕለት ፡ካዕበ ፡-አዕረፈ፡ኢየሱስ ፡ሞአ ፡ አምሳለ ፡ረዳ ፡ ጽጉይ ። ጸሎቱ ወበረከቱ ፡ የሃሉ ፡ ምስሌነ ፡ ለዓለመ ፡ ዓለም ።> ወበዛቲ ፡ ዕለት ፡ ካዕበ ፡ ኮነ ፡ ተዝካሮሙ ፡ ለጌልዎን ፡ ወማይን ፡ ወምክዋስ ፡ ወማርልያስ ። በረከቶሙ ፡ የሀሉ ፡ ምስለ ፡ ንጉሥነ ፡ ኢያሱ ፡ ወምስለ ፡ ፍቁሩ ፡ ዘመንፈስ ፡ ቅዱስ ፡ ለዓለመ ፡ ዓለም ።
in the ages of ages] <On this day also died 'Iyäsus-Mo'a, similar to a rose in bloom. May his prayer and his blessing be with us in the ages of ages.> [On this day also there is the commemoration of Gëlwon, Mäyen, Mekwäs, and Märleyäs. May their blessing be with our king 'Iyäsu and with his dear Za-Manfas Qeddus in the ages of ages]
አመ ፡ ፳5ለኅዳር ፡ ምንባብ ። በስመ ፡ አብ ፡ ወወልድ ፡ ወመንፈስ ፡ ቅዱስ ፡ አሐዱ ፡ አምላክ ። በዛቲ ፡ዕለት ፡ኮነ ፡ ቅዱስ፤ ፡ ያዕቆብ ፡ ሰማዕት ፡ ዘግሙድ" ። ዝንቱ ፡ ቅዱስ ፡ኮነ ፡ እምሐራሁ ፡ ለስክራድ ፡ ወልደ ፡ ሰፉር ፡ ንጉሠ ፡ ፋርስ ። ወኮነ ፡ አርኮ ፡ ወያፈቅሮ ፡ ፈድፋደ ፡ ወይትማከር ፡ ምስሌሁ ፡ በኩሉ ፡ ምግበሩ ። ወበእንተዝ ፡ አጽነነ ፡ ልቦ ፡ ለቅዱስ ፡ ያዕቆብ ፡ 'እምአምልኮተ ፡ ክርስቶስ ፡ ወረሰዮ ፡ ያምልክ ፡ ፀሓየ ፡ ወእሳተ ። ወሶበ ፡ ሰምዓ ፡ እሙ ፡ ወብእሲቱ ፡ ወእኅቱ ፡ ከመ ፡ ውእቱ ፡ ተሰናአወ ፡ ምስለ ፡ ንጉሥ ፡ በምክሩ" ፡ ወበሃይማኖቱ ፡ ጸሐፋ ፡ ወለአካ ፡ ኅቤሁ ፡ እንዘ ፡ ይብላ ፡ ሎቱ ፡ ለም ንት ፤ ኀደገ ፤ እምኔከ ፡ ሃይማኖቶ ፡ ለእግዚእነ ፡ ክርስቶስ ፡ ወተሎከ ፡ ጠባያዓተ ፡ ፍጡራተ ፡ እለ ፡ እሙንቱ?" ፡ ፀሐይ ፤፤! ፡ ወእሳት፤ ። ወአእምር ፡ ይእዜ ፡ እስመ ፡ አንተ ፡ ለእመ ፡ ነበርከ ፡ ውስተ ፡ ሃይማኖቱ ፡ ለንጉሠ ፡ ፋርስ ፡ ንሕነሰ ፡ 'ነኪራነ ፡ ንከውን!" ፡ እምኔከ ። ወሶበ ፡ አንበበ ፡ መጽሐፈ ፡ መልእክቶን ፡ በከየ ፡ ብካየ ፡ መሪረ ፣
[READING] FOR THE 27TH OF HEDAR In the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit, one God. On this day became a martyr the saint Yä'qob (= James) who (was) cut to pieces (= the Intercisus). This saint was among the soldiers of Sekräd (= Yezdegerd), son of Safur (= Sapor) the king of Färs (= Persia). He was his friend and (Sekräd) loved him very much and consulted with him for all his affairs. Because of that, he turned the heart of the saint Yä'qob away from the worship of Christ and made him worship the sun and the fire. When his mother, his wife, and his sister learned that he was in agreement with the king in his sentiment and his faith, they wrote (a letter) and sent (it) to him saying: "Why have you distanced from you the faith of our Lord Christ and have served created beings which are the sun and the fire? Know now that, if you, you remain in the faith of the king of Färs, we will become strangers to you." When he read their letter, he shed bitter tears and said: "If it has happened that, by this, I have become a stranger to my relatives, (to) my family, and (to) my parents, how will I

ወይቤ ፡ ለእመ ፡። ኮነ ፡ በዝንቱ ፡ ኮንኩ፤”፤ ፡ ነኪረ ፡ እምሰብእየ፤ ፡ ወዘመድየ፤ ፡ ወአዝማድየ፤፤ ፣ እፎ ፡ እቀርብ ፡ ኅበ ፡ እግዚእየ ፡ ኢየሱስ ፡ ክርስቶስ" ። ወአኀዘ ፡ ያንብብ ፡ ውስተ፤፤ ፡ መጽሐፈ ፡ ክርስቲያን ፡ ወይበኪ ፡ ወኀደገ፤ ፡ መልእክቶ ፡ ለንጉሥ ። ወነገርዎ ፡ ለንጉሥ ፡ ወይቤልዎ ፡ ዓርክከ ፡ ያዕቆብ ፡ ናሁ ፡ ኀደገ ፡ መልእክተከ ፡ 'ወተመይጠ ፡ እምአምልኮ፤ ፡ አማልክቲሁ ። ወፈነወ ፡፤ ንጉሥ ፡ ወአብጽሖ?' ፡ ኅቤሁ፤ ፡ ወተስእሎ ፡ ወይቤሎ ፡ ለምንት ፡ ኀደገ ፡ መልእክትየ፣ "ወመነንከ ፡ ፍቅርየ ፡ ወአማልክትየ፥፤ ። ወይቤሎ ፡ ቅዱስ ፡ ያዕቆብ ፡ ይቤ ፡ እግዚእነ ፡ ክርስቶስ ፡ በወንጌል ፡ ቅዱስ" ፡ ዘየአምን ፡ ብየ ፡ በቅድመ ፡ ሰብእ ፡ 'አአምኖ ፡ አነ ሂ፥፤ ፡ በቅድመ ፡ አቡየ ፡ ዘበሰማያት ። ወዘሰ ፡ ክሕደኒ ፡ በቅድመ ፡ ሰብእ ፡'እክሕዶ-፡ አነሂ ፡ በቅድመ ፡'አቡየ ፡ ዘበሰማያት፥ ። ወበእን[ተ]ዝ ፡ ኅደጉ ፡ መልእክተከ ፡ ወፍቅረከ ፤ ወአማልክቲከ ፡፣ 'ዘኢኮኑ ፡ አማልክተ፥ ፡ ወእሰግድ ፡ ለእግዚእየ ፡ "ወአምላኪየ ፡ ወመድኃኒየጾ ፡ ኢየሱስ ፡ ክርስቶስ ፡ ወልደ ፡ እግዚአብሔር ፡ ሕያው ፡ ፈጣሬ ፡ ሰማያት ፡ ወምድር ፡ ፀሐይ ፡ ወወርኅ ፡ ወከዋክብት፥" ፡ ወአብሕርት" ። ወሶበ ፡ ሰምዓ ፡ ንጉሥ ፡ ዘንተ ፡ ተምዓ ፡ ፈድፋደ፤ ፡ ወአዘዘ ፡ ከመ ፡ ይቅሥፍዎ ፡ "ቅሥፈተ ፡ዓቢየ፤ ፡ ወቀሠፍዎ፤ ፡ እስከ ፡ ኮነ ፡ ደሙ ፡ ይውሕዝ ፡ ዲበ ፡ ምድር ፣ ወኢተመይጠ ፡ እምክሩ+ ። ወአዘዘ ፡ ንጉሥ ፡ ከመ ፡ ይምትሩ ፡ ኩሎ ፡ መልያልያቲሁ ፡ በመጥባሕታት። ወመተሩ!0" ፡ አጸብዓ ፡ እደዊሁ ፡'ወእደዊሁ ፡ ወአጻብአ ፡ እገሪሁ ፡ ወእገሪሁ ፡ ወመዝራፅሪ[ታ]ቲሁ ፤ ወገመድዎ ፡ በበቋወ፪በበምታር ። ወኩሉ ፡ መልያልይ ፡ ዘይትበተክ ፡ እምኔሁ" ፡ ኮነ፥፤ ፡ ይዜምር ፣ ላዕሌሁ፥ ፡ ወይሴብሖ ፡ ለእግዚአብሔር ፡ ወይቤ ፡ አምላከ ፡ ክርስቲያን ፡ እግዚእየ ፡ ወንጉሥየ ፡ ኢየሱስ ፡ ክርስቶስ ፡ ተወከፍ ፡ ኀቤከ ፡ አፅፁቃቲሃ ፡ ለዕፅ ፡ በከመ ፡ ዕበየ ፡ ሣህልከ ። እስመ ፡ በዓለ ፡ ዓጸደ ፡ ወይን ፡ ሶበ ፡ ይገዝም ፡ ትራፋቲሃ" ፡ ለዓጸደ ፣ ወይኑ ፡ ይእቲሰ ፡፤ ትጸጊ ፡ በወርኃ ፡ ኒሳን ፡ ዝውእቱ ፡ ወርኃ ፡ ሚያዝያ ፡ ወትሠርፅ" ፡ ወትሰፍሕ ፡ ሥርዋቲሃ ። ወሶበ ፡ ተረፈ ፡ እንግድዓሁ ፡ ወርእሱ ፡ ወማእከሌሁ ፡ ወአእመረ ፡ ከመ ፡፤ ጊዜሁ፤ ፡ ቀርበ ፡ 'ከመ ፡ ይሜጡ ፡፣ ነፍሶ ። ወበውእቱ ፡ ጊዜ ፡ ሰአሎ ፡ ለእግዚአብሔር ፡ በእንተ ፡ ዓለም ፡ ወበእንተ ፡ ሕዝብ ፡ ከመ ፡ ይምሀሮሙ ፡ ወይሣሀሎሙ ። ወካዕበ ፡ ይቤ ፡ እስመ ፡ 'አነ ፡ ኢተረፈ ፡ ሊተፆ ፡ እደዊየ" ፡ ከመ ፡ ኣንሥኦሙ ፡፣ ኅቤከ ፤ እግዚኦ ፡ ወናሁ ፡ መልያልያትየ ፡ ግዱፋት ፣ ቅድሜየ" ፡ ተወከፋ ፡ እግዚኦ ፡ ለነፍስየ ። ወሶቤሃ ፡ አስተርአዮ ፡ ሎቱ ፡ እግዚእነ ፡ ክርስቶስ ፡ ወናዘዞ ፡ ወአጽንዖ ፡ 'ወተሐሥየት ፡ ነፍሱ ። ወእምቅድመ ፡ ይመጡ ፡ ነፍሶ ፡ አፍጠነ ፡ 8?፤ወመተረ ፡ ርእሶ ፡ ቅድስተ ፡ በሰይፍ ፡ በሊሕ ፡ ወነሥአ ፡ አክሊለ ፡ ስምዕ ፡
near my Lord Jesus Christ?" 1] He began to read in the books of the Christians and to weep, and he abandoned the king's service. They spoke to the king and said to him: "Behold, your friend Yä'qob has abandoned your service and turned away from the worship of your gods." The king sent for him, brought him before him, questioned him, and said to him: "Why have you abandoned my service and despised my affection and my gods?" The saint Yä'qob said to him: "Our Lord Christ says in the holy Gospel: Whoever will declare himself for me before men, I will also declare myself for him before my Father who is in heaven. But whoever denies me before men, I will also deny him before my Father who is in heaven. Because of this, I have abandoned your service, your affection, and your gods who are not gods, and I worship my Lord, my God, and my Savior, Jesus Christ, Son of the living God, creator of the heavens and the earth, of the sun and the moon, of the stars and the seas." When the king heard this, he became very angry and ordered him to undergo a great scourging. They scourged him until his blood flowed upon the ground, but he did not turn away from his sentiment. The king ordered all his limbs to be cut off with knives. They cut off his fingers and his hands, his toes, his feet, and his arms; they cut him into thirty-two pieces. || He sang over every limb that was severed from him, praised God, and said: "God of the Christians, my Lord and my King, Jesus Christ, receive unto yourself the branches of the tree according to the greatness of your mercy. For, when the master of the vineyard prunes what remains of his vineyard, it blossoms in the month of nisän - that is to say, the month of miyäzyä -, grows, and extends its roots." When there remained only his chest, his head, and his torso, he knew that the moment was approaching to render his soul; at that moment, he prayed to God for the world and for the people (of the Christians) so that He might have pity and mercy upon him. He said again: "Since I have no hands left to raise toward you, Lord, behold my limbs cast before me, receive my soul, Lord." Then our Lord Christ appeared to him, encouraged him, and strengthened him; his soul exulted. Before he rendered his soul, someone hastened to cut off his holy head with a sharpened sword, and he received the crown of martyrdom in the kingdom of heaven. || He went into the luminous dwelling near the Christ whom he loved. God-fearing people took his body, shrouded it in a beautiful shroud, and placed it in a beautiful place. When his mother, his wife, and his sister learned that he had become
በመንግሥተ ፡ ሰማያት ። ወሖረ ፡ ውስተ ፡'ማኅደዶር ፡ ብርሃናዊት" ፡ ኅበ ፡ ክርስቶስ ፡ ዘአፍቀሮ ። ወነሥኡ ፡ ሥጋሁ ፡ ሰብእ ፡ ፈራህያነ ፡ እግዚአብሔር ፡ ወገነዝዎ ፡ ሠናየ ፡ ግንዘተ ፣ ወአንበርዎ ፡ ውስተ ፡ መካን ፣ ሠናይ ። ወሶበ ፡ ሰምዓ ፡ እሙ፣ ወብእሲቱ ፡ ወእኅቱ ፡ ከመ” ፡ ውእቱ ፡ ኮነ ፡ ሰማዕተ ፡ ተፈሥሓ ፡ ዓቢየ ፡ ፍሥሐ ፡ ወመጽኣ ፡ ኀበ ፡ ሀሎ ፡ ሥጋሁ ፡ ወተአምኃሁ?' ፡ ወበከያ” ፡ ላዕሌሁ ፡ ወአንበራ፣ ዲቤሁ" ፡ አልባሳተ ፡ ክቡራተ ፡ ወዕፍረታተ ፡ ምዑዛተ ፡ ብዙኃተ፤ ። ወሶበ ፡ ኮነ ፡ በመዋዕሊሆሙ ፡ ለነገሥት ፡ ጻድቃን ፡ አኖርዮስ ፡ ወአርቃዴዎስ" ፡ ሐነጹ ፡ ሎቱ ፡ ቤተ ፡ ክርስቲያነ ፡ ወደብረ ። ወሰምዓቾ ፡ ንጉሠ ፡ ፋርስ ፡ ዜናሁ ፡ ለውእቱ ፡ ደብር ፡ ወዜናሆሙ ፡ ለሰማዕታት ፡'ወሥጋሆሙ ፡ከመ ፡ በኩሉ ፡ መካን ፡ ያስተርኢ ፡ እምኔሆሙ ፤ ታአምራት ፡ ወመንክራት ፡ ዓበይት ። ወአዘዘ ፡ ከመ ፡ ያውዕዩ ፣ አብድንቲሆሙይጾ ፣ ለሰማዕታት ፡ በኩሉ ፡ መካን ፡ ወኢይኅድጉ ፡ ወኢምንተኒ ፡ አምኔሆሙ ፡ በውስተ ፡ መንግሥቱ ። ወመጽኡ፤ ፡ ቦእምነ፤ ፡ መሃይምናን ፡ ወነሥኡ ፡ ሥጋሁ” ፡ ለቅዱስ ፡ ያዕቆብ ፡ 'ወጾርዎ ፡ ወአብጽሕዎ ፡ ኅበ ፡ ሀገረ ፡ ኢየሩሳሌም ፡ ወአንበርዎ ፡ ኀበ ፡ ጴጥሮስ ፡ ኤኢስ ፡ ቆፅስ ፡ 'ዘሀገረ ፡ ሮሃ" ። ወኮነ ፡ በኀቤሁ ፡ እስከ ፡ አመ ፡ ነግሠ ፡ መርቅያኖስ ። ወበውእቱ ፡ ጊዜ ፡ ነሥኦ ፡ ቅዱስ ፡ ጴጥሮስ ፡ ወአብጽሖ ፡ ኅበ ፡ ብሔረ ፡ ግብጽ ፡ ወአንበርዎ ፡ ውስተ ፡ ሀገረ ፡ ብህንሳ ። ወነበረ ፡ በህየ ፡ ሕዳጠ!" ፡ ወምስሌሁ፤"፤ ፡ አኀው ። ወእንዘ ፡ እሙንቱ ፡ ይጹልዩ ፡ በጊዜ ፣ መንፈቀ ፡ መዓልት፤" ፡ ወሥጋሁ ፡ ለቅዱስ ፡ ያዕቆብ ፡ 'በማእከሌሆሙ ፡ ወሶቤሃ ፣ አስተርአዮሙ ፡ ቅዱስ ፡ ያዕቆብ!" ፡ ምስለ ፡፣ ብዙኃን ፡ ሰማዕት ፡ ዘፋርስ፤" ፡ እንዘ ፡ ይለብሱ ፡ አልባሰ ፡ ሰብአ ፡ ፋርስ ። ወዘመሩ ፡ ምስሌ ሆሙ ፡ ወባረኩ ፡ ላዕሌሆሙ ፡ ወተኃብኡ ፡ እምኔሆሙ! ። ወእምቅድመ ፡ ይሰወሩ ፡ እምኔሆሙ ፡ ይቤሎሙ ፡ ቅዱስ ፡ ያዕቆብ ፡ እስመ ፡ ሥጋየ ፡ የሀሉ ፡ ዝየ ፡ በከመ ፡ አዘዘ፤" ፡ እግዚአብሔር ። ወእምድኅረዝ ፡ ፈቀደ፤‹ ፡ 'ቅዱስ ፡ ጴጥሮስ፤"ጾ ፡ ኤሏስ ፡ ቆፅስ ፡ ከመ ፡ ይትመየጥ ፡ 'ኅኀበ ፡ ብሔሩ ፡ ወነሥአ፤‹ ፡ ሥጋሁ ፡ ለቅዱስ ፡ ያዕቆብ ፡ ምስሌሁ ፡ ወጸሩ፤!" ፡ ኅበ ፡ ባሕር ፡ ወአንበሩ፤፤፤ ፡ ውስተ ፡ ሐመር ፡ ወተዓደወ ፡ቃሎ ፣፡ ለቅዱስ፤!" ፡ ዘይቤ ፡ እስመ ፡ ሥጋየ፤! ፡ የሃሉ ፡ ዝየ ። ወሶቤሃ ፡ ተመስጠ ፡ ሥጋሁ ፡
a martyr, they experienced great joy, came to where his body was, embraced it, wept over it, and placed precious garments and many fragrant perfumes upon it. When it was the time of the righteous emperors 'Anoryos (= Honorius) and 'Argädëwos (= Arcadius), they built a church and a monastery for him. The king of Färs learned the story of this monastery and the story of the martyrs and their bodies: in every place, great miracles and wonders appeared from them. He ordered the remains of the martyrs to be burned in every place and to leave nothing of them in his kingdom. Some of the faithful came, took the body of the saint Yä'qob, carried it away, brought it to the city of Jerusalem, and placed it near Pêtros (= Peter), bishop of the city of Rohä (= Edessa). It remained near him until Margeyänos (= Marcian) reigned. At that time, the saint Pêtros took it and brought it to the land of Egypt; it was placed in the city of Behnesä (= Behnesah). He (= Pêtros) stayed there for some time, with him (were) brothers. While they were praying at the time of midday, the body of the saint Yä'qob (being) among them, then the saint Yä'qob appeared to them with many martyrs of Färs dressed in the garments of the people of Färs. They sang with them, blessed them, and vanished from their (sight). Before (the martyrs) disappeared from their (sight), the saint Yä'qob said to them (= to those present): "Let my body be here just as God has ordered." After that, the saint Pêtros wished to return to his country; he took the body of the saint Yä'qob with him, it was transported to the sea and placed in a ship, and (Pêtros) transgressed the word of the saint who had said: "Let my body be here." Then the body of the saint Yä'qob was taken from their hands and returned to the place where it had dwelt, as God had ordered. Great miracles and wonders occurred from the body of the saint Yä'qob. May his prayer and his blessing be with us, amen.


[p. 384] {#po.44.p384}
ለቅዱስ ፡ ያዕቆብ ፡ እማእከለ ፡ እደዊሆሙ ፡ ወተመይጠ ፡ ኅበ ፡ መካን ፡ ዘነበረ ፡ ውስ ቴቱ ፤ በከመ ፡ አዘዘ ፤ እግዚአብሔር ። ወኮነ ፡ እምሥጋሁ ፡ ለቅዱስ ፡ ያዕቆብ ፡ ተኣምራት ፡ ወመንክራት ፡ ዓበይት ። ጸሎቱ ፡ ወበረከቱ ፡ የሀሉ ፡ ምስሌነ ፡ አሜን ። ወበዛቲ ፡ ዕለት ፡ ካዕበ ፡ ስምዓ ፡ ኮነ ፣ ቅዱስ ፣ ጢሞቴዎስ ፡ ዘእምሰብአ ፡ ብንሑር!፤! ። ወዝንቱ ፡ ቅዱስ ፡ ኮነ ፡ እምንእሱ ፡ ፈራሄ ፡ እግዚአብሔር ፡ ወይጸመድ ፡ "ለጾም ፡ ወለጸሎት ፡ ዘልፈ፤፤ ። ወቦቱ ፡ ብእሲት ፡ እንተ ፡ ስማ ፡ሞራ ፡ ወለተ ፡እኅወ፣ አቡሁ ፡ ወይእቲ ፡ ሠናይት ፡ጥቀ ፡ በኩሉ፤፤ ፡ ወታሠምሮ ፡ በምግባራ፤! ። ወነበሩ ፡ ብዙኃ ፡ መዋዕለ ፡ በፍቅር ፡ ወበተሠናእዎ ። ወእንዘ ፡ ሀለዉ ፡ ከመዝ ፡ በኅድአት ፡
[On this day also became a martyr the saint Timotëwos (= Timothy) who (was) of the people of Benhur. This saint feared God from his childhood and he devoted himself unceasingly to fasting and prayer. || He had a wife whose name (was) Morä, daughter of his father's brother. She was very beautiful in every (respect) and pleased him by her way of acting. They lived for many days in affection and concord. While they were thus in tranquility, an edict of the emperor Diyogletyänos (= Diocletian) the impious was promulgated in

all the countries of his empire saying: "Let the temples of idols be opened and let the churches be closed." When the saint Timotëwos learned this, he rejoiced greatly, for he was awaiting this moment. He told his wife how he was going to act. She said to him: "May the will of God be done." Then he arose and prayed that God might secure his path. As he was sleeping that night, God appeared to him and said to him: "Peace be to you, Timotëwos my friend, behold I have counted you among the number of the righteous. Now, arise, take your wife, go to the city of Behnesä (= Behnesah), and confess my name before all the people who worship idols." (Once) awake, he told his wife how he had seen (God). She told him her (own) dream which (was) the same. Then, they arose together, arrived before Quelquelyänos (= Culcianus) the governor, and found him celebrating a feast with masanqo and 'enzirä. Then the soldiers seized him and placed him before him; as for his wife, she followed him. The governor spoke to him with gentle words in (seeking to) lead him to worship idols. But the saint cursed him and his impure faith. Because of this, the governor became angry and ordered him to be thrown into prison. A few days later, the governor brought him out with his wife and (had them) scourged with whips until their blood flowed like water. His (= Timotëwos') wife ended her life in this torture; as for the saint Timotëwos, (the governor) undertook again to treat him with gentleness, wanting to persuade him to worship idols. As he refused (it) to him, the governor ordered his body to be coated with oil and resin and he was thrown into a furnace of fire. The angel of God Gabr'ël (= Gabriel) descended from heaven to him and saved him from this torture. Then, he was thrown back into prison and he performed many miracles and wonders there. One day, while he was praying there, our Lord Jesus Christ came, made a covenant with him, and gave him many greetings. The next day, they brought him out of the prison, led him to the city of Benhur, and cut off his holy head. || There was then a shaking and the air was filled with angels; the people of the city came, shrouded him in a beautiful shroud, and buried him. When the time of the persecution had passed, they built a church for him, they consecrated it on the twenty-seventh of sanë, and they placed his holy body there. May his blessing be with our king 'lyäsu and with his dear Za-Manfas Qeddus in the centuries of centuries.]
በረከቱ ፡ ተሀሉ ፡ ምስለ ፡ ንጉሥነ ፡ ኢያሱ ፡ ወምስለ ፡ ፍቁሩ ፡ ዘመንፈስ ፡ ቅዱስ ፡ ለዓለመ ፡ ዓለም ። ወበዛቲ ፡ ዕለት ፡ ካዕበ ፡ ተዝካሩ ፤ ለቅዱስ ፡ ፊልሞን፤ ፡ ሐዋርያ ፡ ዘእምድወ፪አር ድእት፤ « ጸሎቱ ፡ ወበረከቱ ፡ የሀሉ ፡ ምስሌነ ፡ ለዓለመ ፡ ዓለም ፡ አሜን ።
On this day also (is) the commemoration of the saint Filmon (= Philemon) the apostle who (is among the number) of the seventy-two disciples. May his prayer and his blessing be with us in the centuries of centuries, amen.

[p. 388] {#po.44.p388}
ወበዛቲ ፡ ዕለት ፡ ካዕበ ፡ ተዝካሮሙ ፡ ለአርአሬ፤፥ ፡ ወአባ ፡ ተክለ ፡ ሐዋርያት ፡ ዘገበርማ ፡ ወቀሌምንጦስ ፡ ሰማዕት ፡ ዘእንደርብጎይ፤ ፡ ምድረ ፡ ዘርቦች ። በረከቶሙ ፡ የሀሉ ፡ ምስለ ፡ ፍቁሩ ፡ ዘመንፈስ ፡ ቅዱስ ፡ ለዓለመ ፡ ዓለም ።
[On this day also (is) the commemoration of 'Ar'arë, (of) abbä Takla hawäryät of Gabarmä and (of) Qalëmentos (= Clement) the martyr of 'Endarbegoy in the land of Zarbotch. May their blessing be with his (= of King 'lyäsu) dear Za-Manfas Qeddus in the centuries of centuries.]

### READING OF THE 28 HEDAR

In the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit, one God. On this day the saint Säräbämon (= Sarapamon), bishop of the city of Nagiyos (= Nikiou), became a martyr. This saint was of the family of the saint 'Estifänos (= Stephen), of the tribe of Lëwi (= Levi), of the city of Jerusalem. The name of his father was 'Abrehäm (= Abraham), son of Lëwi, son of Yosëf (= Joseph), brother of Sem'on (= Simeon), brother of the mother of 'Estifanos. At the moment of his birth, they called him by the name of his father's father. When his parents were dead, he desired to become a Christian. An angel of God appeared to him and ordered him to go to the bishop abbä Yohannes. He went to him and (the latter) revealed to him the mysteries of the incarnation of Christ God, but he did not dare to baptize him with the Christian baptism in the city of Jerusalem for fear of the Jews, his relatives. The bishop reflected on what he could do for him. Our Lady, the holy Virgin Mary, appeared to him (= to Sarapamon) and ordered him to go to the city of Alexandria to the patriarch abba Tëwonäs (= Theonas). While he was going, an angel of God approached him in the appearance of a man and walked with him until he had arrived at the city of Alexandria. The angel of God had previously informed the patriarch about the saint Säräbämon. The patriarch rejoiced when he arrived before him, he taught him the way of God and baptized him with the holy Christian baptism. Then (Sarapamon) became a monk in the monastery of the saint abbä Säwiros (= Severus) which (was) outside the city of Alexandria. When the patriarch abbä Tëwonäs died, they instituted abbä Pêtros (= Peter) in his place. (The latter) sent for and brought this saint Saräbämon to him to help him in patriarchal affairs. After that, he consecrated him bishop of the city of Niqyos, and the churches rejoiced greatly at it. God manifested miracles and wonders through his hands. There were near his city temples of idols where worship was practiced and he prayed to our Lord Christ to destroy them. God accepted his prayer: the earth swallowed them and the river rose above them and covered them. Because of his prayer, God annihilated the worship of idols in the territory of his diocese. (Sarapamon) broke the blasphemy of Sabelyos (= Sabellius) who made the Father, the Son, and

the Holy Spirit one person (= helläwë). When Diyogletyänos (= Diocletian) abjured, they spoke to him and said: "The saint Sarabämon has abolished the worship of idols, the gods of the emperor." When the emperor learned this, he became very angry and ordered him to be brought to him. When the saint Säräbämon had arrived at the city of Alexandria with the emperor's envoys, the patriarch abbä Pêtros came to him and, with him, many priests, and they greeted him. They saw (that) his face (was) like (that) of an angel of God. When he arrived before the emperor Diyogletyänos, (the latter) (had) him tortured with many kinds of torture. Our Lord Christ raised him up unharmed, without suffering or damage. Many people believed because of him; the emperor feared (that), if he increased his torture, many (other) people would believe because of him; he sent him to Upper Egypt to 'Aryänos (= Arianus), the governor of the city of 'Andënäw (= Antinoë) so that he might (have) him tortured and that they might cut off his head. At that moment, 'Aryänos was in the city of Alexandria. 'Aryänos took the saint Saräbämon with him in a boat to go to Upper Egypt. When the boat had arrived at Niqyos, his (= Sarapamon's) city, the boat stopped and they could not make it move from its place. They brought the saint Saräbämon out of the boat, led him to the north of his city, cut off his head with a sword, and he received the crown of martyrdom in the kingdom of heaven. His people took his holy body with great honors, carried it to the church, and placed it there. Great miracles and wonders occurred from him. May his prayer and his blessing be with us forever, amen.
ምስሌነ ፡ ለዓለም ፡ አሜን ። <ወበዛቲ ፡ ዕለት ፡ ካዕበ ፡ ኮኑ ፡ አብርሃም ፡ ወይስሐቅ ፡ ወያዕቆብ ። ጸሎቶሙ ፡ ቄናጽል ፡ በሀገረ?" ፡ ኢትዮጵያ ። በረከቱ ፡ ተሀሉ ፡ ምስለ ፡ ንጉሥነ ፡ ኢያሱ ፡ ለዓለመ ፡ ዓለም ። <ወበዛቲ ፡ ዕለት ፡ ካዕበ ፡ ኮኑ ፤ አብርሃም ፡፤ ወይስሐቅ ፡ ወያዕቆብ ። ጸሎቶሙ ፡ ወበረከቶሙ ፡ የሃሉ ፡ ምስለ ፡ ፍቁሮሙ ፡ ለዓለመ ፡ ዓለም ።>
[On this day also (is) the commemoration of abbä Ligänos the priest, who fought (spiritually) in the monastery of Quanäsel in the land of Ethiopia. May his blessing be with our king 'lyäsu in the centuries of centuries.] On this day also there are Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob. May their prayer and their blessing be with their friend in the centuries of centuries.


[p. 392] {#po.44.p392}

### READING OF THE 29 HEDAR
አመ ፡ ፳8ለኅዳር ፡ ምንባብ ። በስመ ፡ አብ ፡ ወወልድ ፡ ወመንፈስ ፡ ቅዱስ ፡ አሐዱ ፡ አምላክ ። በዛቲ ፡ ዕለት ፡ ኮነ ፡ ቅዱስ ፡ ጴጥሮስ ፡ ሰማዕት ፡ ሊቀ ፡ ጳጳሳት ፡ ዘሀገረ ፡ እስክንድ ርያ ፡ ተፍጻሜ፤ ፡ ሰማዕት ። ወውእቱ ፡ እምፕልቆሙ ፡ ለአበው ፡ ሊቃነ ፡ ጳጳሳት ፡ ፲ወ፪ ። ለዝንቱ ፡ አብ ፡ ኮነ ፡ አቡሁ ፡ ሊቀ ፡ ካህናት ፡ ዘሀገረ ፡ እስክንድርያ ፡ ወስሙ ፡ ቴዎዶስዮስ ፡ ወስማ ፡ ለእሙ ፡ ሶፊያ ። ወኮኑ ፡ ፈራህያነ ፡ እግዚአብሔር ፡ ፈድ ፋደ" ፡ ወኢኮነ ፡ ሎሙ ፡ ወልደ ። 'ወሶበ ፡ ኮነ ፡ አመ ፡ ጸለሐምሌ ፡ በዓሎሙ፣ ለቅዱሳን ፡ ጴጥሮስ ፣ ወጳውሎስ ፡ ሐዋርያት!" ፡ ርእየት ፡ እሙ ፡ ለዝንቱ ፡ ቅዱስ ፡ ብዙኃነ ፡ ክርስቲያነ ፡ ወውሉዶሙ ፡ ምስሌሆሙ ፡ ስርግዋን ፡ በአልባሲሆሙ ። 'ወእሙ ንቱ ፡እንዘ ፡ የሐውሩ፤! ፡ ኅበ ፡ ቤተ ፡ ክርስቲያን ። ወሐዘነት፤ ፡ ፈድፋደ ፡ ወቦአት ፤ ውስተ ፡ ቤተ ፡ ክርስቲያን ፤ ወቆመት! ፡ በቅድመ ፡ ምሥዋዕ ፡ ቅዱስ ፡ ወሰአለቶ ፡ "ለእግዚእነ ፡ ክርስቶስ ፡ በብዙኅ ፡ ብካይ፤፤ ፡ ከመ ፡ የሀባ ፡ ወልደ ። ወበይእቲ ፤ ሌሊት ፡ አስተርአዩ ፡ ላቲ ፡ ጴጥሮስ ፤ ወጳውሎስ ፡ ሐዋርያት ፡፣ ወይቤልዋ ፡ ናሁ ፡ እግዚአብሔር ፤ ተወክፈ፤?-! ፡ ስእለተኪ ፡ ወሀሎ ፡ ከመ ፡ ይሁበኪ ፡ ወልደ ፡ ወትሰምዬ ፡ ስሞ ፡ ጴጥሮስ ። ወአዘዝዋ ፡ ከመ ፡ ትሖር ፡ ኅበ ፡ ሊቀ ፡ ጳጳሳት ፡ አባ ፣ ቴዎናስ! ፡ ከመ ፡ ይጹሊ ፡ ላዕሌሃ ። ወእምድኅረ ፡ ሕዳጥ ፡ መዋዕል?! ፡ ወለደቶ ፡ ለዝንቱ ፡ ቅዱስ ፣ ወሰመየቶ፤" ፡ ጴጥሮስ ። ወሶበ ፡ ኮነ ፡ መዋዕሊሁ ፡ ዓመት ፣ መጠውዎ”፤ ፡ ለሊቀ ፡ ጳጳሳት ፡ በከመ ፡ ሳሙኤል ፡ ነቢይ ። ወኮነ ፡ ሎቱ ፡ በከመ ፡ "ወልድ ፡ ፍቁር ፡ ወሜሞ ፡ አናጐንስጢሰ ። 'ወእምድኅረ ፡ ሕዳጥ ፡ መዋዕል ፡ ጫሞ ፡ ዲያቆነ ፡ 'ወእምድኅረ ፡ ሕዳጥ ፡ መዋዕል?" ፡ ጫሞ ፡ ቀሲሰ ። ወኮነ ፡ ይትራድኦ ፡ በምግባራት ፡ ብዙኃት ፡ 'እምግባራተ ፡ ቤተ፦፥0 ፡ ክርስቲያናት ። ወሶበ ፡ አዕረፈ ፡ ሊቀ ፡ ጳጳሳት ፡ አባ ፡ ቴዎናስ ፡ አዘዞሙ ፡ ለኤኢስ ፡ ቆፅሳት ፡ ወለካህናት ፡ ከመ ፡ ይሚምዎ ፡ ለዝንቱ ፡ አብ ፡ ጴጥሮስ ፡ ሊቀ ፡ ጳጳሳተ ፡ ህየንቴሁ፣ ወሜምዎ ፡ ሊቀ ፡ ጳጳሳተ፤። ፡ ላዕለ ፡ ሀገረ ፤ እስክንድርያ ። ወሶበ ፣ ሜምዎ ፡ ተፈሥሑ ፡ ቦቱ ፡ ኩሎሙ ፡ ሕዝብ ፡ ወኩሎሙቶ ፡ አብያተ ፡ ክርስቲያናትቶ፤ ። ወኮነ ፡'ዝንቱ ፡ ቅዱስ ፡ በመዋዕሊሁ ፡ ለዲዮቅልጥያኖስ" ፡ ከሓዲ ። ወሀሎ ፡ በሀገረ ፡ አንጾኪያ ፡ ፳መስፍን ፡ ተሰናአወ ፡ ምስለ ፡ ንጉሥ ፡ ወሰገደ ፡ ለጣዖታት ፡ ምስሌሁ ። ወሀለዎ ፡ ፪ውሉድ ፡፣ ወኮነት ፡ ብእሲቱ ፡ ክርስቲያናዊተ ፡ ወኢተክህላ ፡ ከመ ፡ ታጥምቆሙ ፡ ለውሉዳ ፡ ጥምቀተ ፡ ክርስትና ፡ በሀገረ ፡ አንጾኪያ ። ወበእንተዝ?፥ ፡ ነሥአቶሙ ፡ ወተጽዕነት ፡ ዲበ ፡ ሐመር ፡ 'ከመ ፡ ትምጻእ ፡ ኀበ ፡ ሀገረ ፡ እስክንድርያ ። ወእንዘ ፡ የሐውሩ ፡ ውስተ ፡ ባሕር ፣ ተንሥአ ፡ ላዕሌሆሙ ፡ ማእበለ ፡ ባሕር ፤ ወፈርሃት ፡ ከመ ፣ ኢይሙቱ፥፤ ፡ ውሉዳ ፡ ዘእንበለ ፡ ጥምቀት= ። ወሰጠ
In the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit, one God. On this day became a martyr the saint Pëtros (= Peter) patriarch of the city of Alexandria, he who completes (the series) of the martyrs. Among the number of the patriarch fathers he is the seventeenth. The father of this father was archpriest of the city of Alexandria, his name (was) Téwodosyos (= Theodosius); the name of his mother (was) Sofiya (= Sophia). They feared God greatly, but had no child. When it was the fifth of hamilë, the feast of the saints Peter and Paul the apostles, the mother of this saint saw many Christians and their children with them, adorned in their clothes; they were going to the church. She was greatly afflicted, entered the church, stood before the holy altar, and begged our Lord Christ with many tears to give her a son. That night, the apostles Peter and Paul appeared to her and said to her: "Behold, God has accepted your prayer; he is going to give you a son and you shall call (him) by the name of Pêtros." They ordered her to go to the patriarch abbä Téwonäs (= Theonas) so that he might pray for her. A short time later, she gave birth to this saint and called him Pëtros. When his days were seven years, his parents handed him over to the patriarch like Sämu'ël (= Samuel) the prophet. He was for him like a beloved son and (the patriarch) ordained him reader. A short time later, he ordained him deacon and, a short time later, he ordained him priest. (Pêtros) helped him in many affairs among the affairs of the churches. At the moment of dying, the patriarch abbä Tëwonäs prescribed to the bishops and the priests to institute this father Pêtros patriarch in his place, and they instituted him patriarch of the city of Alexandria. When they had instituted him, all the people and all the churches rejoiced because of him. This saint lived in the time of Diyogletyänos (= Diocletian) the impious. || There was in the city of 'Ansokiyä (= Antioch) a judge; he was in agreement with the emperor and worshipped idols with him. He had two sons and his wife was a Christian. She had not been able (to have) her sons baptized with the Christian baptism in the city of 'Ansokiyä. Because of this, she took them and embarked on a boat to go to the city of Alexandria. While they were going on the sea, the waves of the sea rose against them. She was afraid that her sons might die without baptism. She tore her breasts, traced the sign of the cross with her blood on the forehead of her sons, and baptized them in the sea three times in the name of the holy Trinity, the Father and the Son and the Holy Spirit. After that, there was a great calm, they were
